the original 1,024-record bound, and directories exceeding the scan or byte
limits still fail closed. A CPU fixture with 1,024 invalid decoys and one
verified target demonstrates this bounded behavior; it does not establish
real-model use or decision improvement.

Source-session receipt verification is also bounded per retrieval: at most four
distinct source sessions are loaded, each through the existing 8 MiB session-file
limit, for a maximum reserved read budget of 32 MiB and no more than four cached
session payloads. Repeated records from a cached source session do not spend another
reservation. Candidates beyond that bound are recorded as deferred and omitted as
unverified; they are not labeled invalid or allowed to affect selection. A CPU-only
counterfactual checks this distinction. The bound is a conservative reservation
based on each file's maximum size, not a measure of actual bytes read. Real-model
application remains untested.

The ordinary `PersistentConversationService` prompt path now uses the same source
receipt verifier before canonical memories enter a normal chat prompt. Exact
transcript recall and check-ins that intentionally suppress memory do not scan
retained records. For other requests, records with invalid or deferred source
verification are omitted before the four-record selection cap, and the ordinary
prompt's retrieval status reports partial verification. A deterministic fake
invoker counterfactual places four newer, altered records ahead of one valid
retained statement: raw lexical retrieval selects the altered records, while the
interactive chat path supplies only the receipt-verified statement and the fake
decision changes accordingly. This establishes source-governed context assembly
for both ordinary chat and resident cognition; it does not establish real-model
interpretation, current truth, or improved outcomes. Direct low-level
`CanonicalMemoryStore.retrieve` calls without a validator remain unverified
retrieval results and must not be treated as prompt-safe evidence.

Ordinary chat forwards the existing resident bridge's pinned semantic-memory
configuration to verified canonical-memory retrieval and the pinned semantic
history configuration to developmental-history retrieval. Bounded semantic
scores can admit lexically disjoint paraphrases into developmental-history
ranking, but lexical overlap remains the primary sort key and semantic score
breaks ties; a lexical match may therefore still outrank a stronger paraphrase.
Canonical-memory receipt validation still filters records before semantic
candidate selection. Developmental-history selection binds the pinned embedder
identity and semantic selection inputs in its retrieval-policy ID; derived
vectors remain a bounded cache and do not become evidence. If a configuration
is absent or an encoder is unavailable, the relevant retrieval path falls back
to its existing lexical behavior. This describes selection mechanics, not
embedding quality, model interpretation, or improved outcomes.
The proposed [semantic developmental-history retrieval experiment](semantic_developmental_history_retrieval_experiment.md)
separates a retrieval-only relevance gate from any later matched response
experiment; neither phase has been performed for developmental-history
semantic retrieval. A first immutable Phase C preregistration helper now freezes
disjoint deterministic acquisition and delayed-probe tasks plus a minimum-delay
contract. It also verifies supplied digest-only fixture outcomes and computes
paired delayed-probe correctness deltas. A pre-effect guard can enforce a
minimum receipt-completion interval before a probe call. Its gate record is
persisted in owner-side custody bound to the final invocation receipt and
request; verification requires the exact gate digest in the delayed outcome.
The module now also provides `DurableDevelopmentalLearningRunner` for resumable
acquisition and delayed-probe execution over a persisted protocol, plus a
stored-chat receipt verifier that binds prompt and output digests to the
conversation transcript, serving receipt, and pre-effect evidence. Acquisition
history and scored-feedback exposure are re-resolved through injected source
verifiers; a production runtime factory wires these owners without starting
inference. Protocol v6 assigns each task's condition order from a nonce-derived
task-digest ranking, balanced across both orders; protocol v5 keeps its original
alternating schedule when reverified. The `NO_FEEDBACK_POLICY_DIGEST` permits a
first matched Phase C study before any scored Phase C outcome exists, with the
empty feedback set bound and checked throughout prompt, receipt, and outcome
verification. Scored-feedback policies still require nonempty source-verified
records. These mechanisms address schedule assignment and first-run
bootstrapping, but do not attest independent scorer operation, authenticate the
operator-supplied nonce, or establish external clock custody. No Phase C run or
learning result is documented; controlled production execution and empirical
evidence remain outstanding.
Protocol v7 remains limited to receipt-bound exposure and descriptive delayed
performance. Its branch IDs do not route delayed probes to isolated state or
orchestrate post-acquisition writeback. Protocol v10 adds condition-owned state
and transcript branches, receipt-bound consolidation, bounded strategy
projections with a preregistered answer-exclusion audit, and delayed-probe
consumption gates. Its v6 branch preflight now
seals state, conversation, and writeback root identities and rejects local
cross-branch storage overlap. These are local custody checks, not independent
filesystem or process isolation attestations. V10 therefore still reports
`retention_contrast_eligible=false`; no V10 Phase C run or retention result is
documented, and the remaining independent isolation and answer-exclusion
evidence has not been established. See the
[condition-branch design](durable_learning_condition_branch_design.md) for the
implemented path and its remaining eligibility requirements.
Protocol v11 adds an opt-in preregistered minimum for correct withheld-condition
acquisition probes; the frozen denominator counts probe presentations across
acquisition tasks, even when tasks repeat an input. After verified acquisition
and before any delayed probe,
the runner persists a digest-bound, answer-free competence assessment. An
unscoreable or below-threshold baseline stops delayed execution; resume,
finalization, and the owner-side evaluation store reload and reconstruct the
same assessment before accepting delayed evidence. The persistence boundary
also rejects a delayed outcome without a qualified assessment and rejects
persisting a delayed-probe start gate or invocation attempt before that
assessment exists. It also rejects creating the assessment after a delayed
outcome or invocation attempt exists. Independent outcome-set verification
applies the same qualification rule. V11 outcome-set and evaluation reports
use schema v34 to include the assessment and direct authorization references;
independent outcome-set and evaluation verification now require the full
authorization receipt set. V5-v10 retain the v32 report shape.
The [evaluation v32 schema](durable_learning_evaluation_v32.schema.json)
describes the compatibility envelope, and the
[evaluation v33 schema](durable_learning_evaluation_v33.schema.json)
records the earlier assessment-only envelope. The
[evaluation v34 schema](durable_learning_evaluation_v34.schema.json) requires
authorization references bound to each delayed attempt; metric correctness
and digest reconstruction remain the runtime verifier's responsibility.
The standalone [outcome-set report schema](durable_learning_outcome_set_v34.schema.json)
reuses the same v34 report definition embedded in that evaluation envelope.
This classifies baseline task competence only; it does not show
that history caused later performance, establish durable learning, or confer
authority. Protocols v5-v10 retain their original contracts and identifiers.
Call `make_durable_learning_protocol` with public argument
`protocol_schema=PROTOCOL_SCHEMA_V11` and a study-specific
`minimum_withheld_acquisition_correct_probe_count` to opt in.
The answer-free assessment artifact shape is specified by the
[competence assessment JSON Schema](durable_learning_competence_assessment.schema.json);
schema validation complements, but does not replace, digest reconstruction
against stored outcomes. The read-only retained-gate inventory schema v5 now
reports acquisition and delayed-probe counts, invocation attempts, evaluation
presence, authorization receipt references, and whether the assessment is not
applicable, not yet assessable, missing, or present with its qualification and
score counts. It does not turn execution progress into a learning claim.
V11 remains unrun: the threshold is now enforceable in the protocol, but its
value still needs preregistration for a specific study and the independent
scoring and isolation requirements remain outstanding.
This is distinct from the rootless Hermes cognition cycles, which have their
own persisted, objectively scored observations. Earlier cycles showed
acquisition without transfer and unstable restoration. A new source-bound
cycle on 2026-11-21 corrected the objective-family projection bound and reached
disjoint transfer: acquisition scored 3/3 in all conditions, while transfer
scored 0/3 in all three. A later custody audit found that the transfer prompt
projection had zero task-relevant records/examples in both history-present and
history-restored, despite selecting the acquisition record. The compact stored
score rows had dropped the probe results that the task-family reader required
to admit the retained examples. This makes the transfer comparison
unqualified; the score tie is not evidence for or against history use. The
projection now retains the validated history-present probe rows, and the
pre-inference path fails closed if a history-required prompt has no relevant
record or if a selected task-relevant experience is absent from the exact
request-bound prompt. A separate embodied decision probe was
ineligible because the record did not match its preregistered consequence
pattern; the companion-history comparison also rejected an invalid withheld
response. See the
[Hermes cycle evidence and limitations](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md)
for run identities, scoring records, and protocol details. Future work should
test how retained experience can change a disjoint decision while keeping
correctness restoration, output restoration, and eligibility as separate
measurements.
`tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_chat_semantic_retrieval_applies_only_verified_paraphrased_memory`
uses deterministic vectors to show a paraphrased older memory displacing a
newer weak lexical match in ordinary chat, verifies the unavailable-encoder
fallback, and rejects a tampered source record. This demonstrates the software
path with controlled substitutes; it does not measure real embedding quality,
real-model interpretation, or improved outcomes.

Ordinary chat now uses the existing bounded exchange-aware selector for
same-session dialogue when no narrower recall, check-in, self-contained, or
runtime-context policy applies. Relevant complete exchanges are considered
before recent dialogue fills the configured character budget, with at most 64
candidate turns. The deterministic
`tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_chat_uses_relevant_older_exchange_before_recent_fill`
counterfactual shows recent-only reconstruction omitting an earlier decision,
then ordinary chat selecting that source-linked exchange and changing a fake
decision while preserving the same budget. This is controlled retrieval and
application evidence; real-model interpretation remains unverified.

When resident cognition cannot handle a turn, ordinary chat can now consume an
already-enabled epistemic projection through the same complete-packet binder
used by resident cognition. The bridge applies the question relevance bound,
temporal freshness review, omission accounting, and source-bound packet format;
ordinary chat omits this context when epistemic consumption is disabled, on
narrow recall/check-in/runtime requests, or in the compact gaming prompt. The
projection is historical, non-authoritative, read-only context and does not
activate calibration review or revise epistemic state. The CPU counterfactual
in `tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_fallback_applies_bounded_prior_epistemic_context`
shows the supplied position changing a fake ordinary-chat decision.
`::test_bridge_binds_only_verified_prior_epistemic_context` checks that the
existing verified packet reaches this adapter and leaves admitted state
unchanged; `::test_local_chat_prompt_rejects_epistemic_context_with_authority_flags`
checks prompt assembly rejects self-authorizing flags. Real-model application
and improved longitudinal outcomes remain unverified.

When an ordinary fallback includes the projection, its selection digest is
also carried in the governed invocation linkage and saved assistant-turn
linkage. This gives later review a durable identity for the prior packet that
was presented alongside that response, without claiming that the model relied
on it. Responses without a selected packet do not receive this field. The
ordinary fallback counterfactual checks that the digest matches in both
places and is absent when no projection is selected.

The ordinary-chat history selection now also binds the selection digest to the
target model identity digest used for model-aware ranking. The assistant-turn
linkage carries that digest, and stored-invocation recovery recomputes it from
the receipt-verified serving identity before accepting the exposure. This
establishes which model identity the retrieval request targeted; it does not
establish model reliance, transfer, or cognitive continuity. Legacy v1
selection packets remain accepted without this identity binding. Response
exposures use schema v4 for target-identity binding; schema v5 additionally
preserves the owner history-state digest in later operator-feedback context.
The operator-feedback route verifies the original
stored invocation and its serving identity before it copies that exposure into
a new feedback record, keeping the model-target binding attached to the cited
assistant response.

New ordinary-chat history selection schema v3 also binds the owner's durable
history-state digest at selection time. The digest follows the selected record
references through the governed invocation linkage, saved assistant turn, and
later operator-feedback exposure (schema v5). This identifies the exact
reconciled history inventory from which the packet was selected; it does not
indicate which records influenced the answer or prove learning.

For sparse ordinary fallback questions, epistemic packet ranking also receives
the latest digest-verified prior user topic from the already selected session
history. Short acknowledgments and turns with invalid text digests do not
replace that cue; specific questions remain unchanged. The deterministic
`tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_fallback_applies_bounded_prior_epistemic_context`
counterfactual shows the same sparse follow-up receiving the west-sensor prior
only when that topic exists in history. The portable
`::test_sparse_ordinary_epistemic_query_uses_only_latest_verified_topic` checks
the digest and acknowledgment filters. This demonstrates query construction
and prompt application with controlled substitutes; it does not establish
real-model interpretation or improved outcomes.

When the latest prior user turn is itself sparse or elliptical, ordinary
epistemic ranking also includes its immediately preceding assistant question
when that turn's text digest verifies and it contains a question mark. The
assistant text is only a topic cue;
it is not treated as evidence or authority, and invalid assistant digests are
ignored. `tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_sparse_ordinary_epistemic_query_resolves_elliptical_answer_antecedent`
shows a verified question plus "That one" selecting the west-sensor prior while
the answer alone does not, and confirms a tampered antecedent is excluded. The
real-owner counterfactual in
`tests/test_resident_developmental_cognition.py::test_sparse_session_topic_changes_real_prior_epistemic_projection_selection`
also confirms the resolved query changes selection among persisted epistemic
propositions.

The ordinary fallback budgets this optional epistemic projection against the
complete chat prompt. It reserves framing space when asking the shared packet
binder to select prior state, then checks the serialized prompt against the
governed 8,000-character limit before keeping the projection. If there is not
enough room, the projection is omitted so it cannot make an otherwise
admissible ordinary prompt oversized. The CPU test
`tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_epistemic_context_uses_only_remaining_prompt_budget`
checks that a bounded prior packet remains available under the total prompt
limit, while `::test_ordinary_epistemic_context_is_skipped_without_prompt_headroom`
checks the no-headroom boundary. This establishes prompt-budget compatibility
with a controlled bridge; real-model interpretation and improved outcomes
remain unverified.

Ordinary fallback can also receive bounded developmental history through the
existing resident owner. The owner performs the existing durable-record
verification and question-relevance ranking, then emits compact packets bound
to exact record and digest identities. This ordinary-chat path deliberately
uses lexical retrieval only, so it does not invoke a configured semantic
embedding model; insufficient prompt headroom, disabled ownership, or an empty
verified projection leaves the prompt unchanged. Context assembly checks the
selection and packet identities and rejects authority flags or tampering. The
packet is historical experience, not current evidence, policy, an instruction,
or permission to update any state. Selected identities and the selection digest
are saved on the invocation and assistant turn. The model-aware owner entry
point receives the active serving identity, allowing scored task-family
examples to carry exact same-model, different-model, or unbound applicability
posture. Missing identity remains explicitly unbound and does not disable
history retrieval. This describes provenance and does not imply successful
transfer. The prompt treats these relations as applicability metadata, not
performance evidence. The provider also accepts an optional bounded record
allowlist and matching record-digest allowlist for controlled callers. The
owner revalidates that every requested record is in completed history and that
each reloaded digest matches before projecting it. The returned selection
identifies the allowlist policy and any records omitted for prompt budget, so a
future ordinary-chat intervention can verify that present and restored
conditions used the same exact content.

Ordinary chat currently consumes verified developmental history but does not
itself write a developmental record: the chat response contract binds
`developmental_history_writeback_performed` to false, and transcript retention
is a separate store and policy. A chat turn or assistant answer is therefore
not automatically treated as a learned experience. A separate local,
operator-confirmed developmental-cycle route can take bounded operator feedback
about a verified chat exchange, bind it to the cited conversation turns, capture
a fresh screen observation, and submit the resulting report through the existing
governed writeback owner. The cycle response returns the report's stable
content-derived source ID and assertion digest so an operator can correlate it
with later audit records without echoing the assertion text. The feedback is
recorded as an unverified report; its presence or successful writeback does not
establish that the system learned.
Evidence of development still requires a later scored follow-up. Ordinary
history retrieval alone establishes none of these steps.

The CPU-only
`verify_ordinary_chat_history_projection_triplet` helper validates both
projection digests, the available/selected/omitted record partition, its
prompt-budget omission digest and reason, and confirms the withheld context is
absent. Its result explicitly leaves effect and learning unestablished.
`build_ordinary_chat_history_intervention_prompts` assembles the three prompts
from one fixed context argument set, verifies present and restored prompts are
identical, and checks that removing only the developmental-history block from
the present prompt yields the withheld prompt. It binds digests for each full
prompt, the shared non-history prompt, and the exact inserted history block,
along with the intended condition order. Legacy deterministic bundles use
`sentientos.ordinary_chat_history_intervention_prompts:v3`; newly prepared
seeded bundles use v4. Each bundle includes one digest that binds the prompt
digests and verification metadata. Legacy bundles retain their deterministic
cycle through all six condition permutations,
placing each condition in each position twice per six-trial block. Protocol v6
uses a pseudorandomized ordering of those six permutations in each block: every
complete block still contains each order exactly once, while an immutable
randomization-seed artifact chooses the order within the block. The seed is
persisted before inference, and the schedule cohort digest, prompt bundle, and
protocol bind its digest. Reload verifies the persisted seed artifact against
the protocol rather than regenerating a missing seed. Previously prepared
bundles remain auditable under their original deterministic or alternating
schedule. This randomizes condition order while preserving position balance; it
does not establish a causal effect or learning. The durable runner persists and
assigns trial indices across trials.
`verify_ordinary_chat_history_intervention_prompt_bundle`
revalidates the prompt and schedule bindings after handoff. The bundle returns
prompt digests, not model outputs. This is prompt-control preparation, not yet
an inference runner or scored result. The deterministic
`tests/test_persistent_governed_conversation.py::test_ordinary_fallback_applies_verified_developmental_history`
counterfactual demonstrates a changed fake fallback answer when the relevant
verified history is present, and the paired prompt test checks tampering is
rejected. This proves the software path and its controlled decision effect;
real-model use, beneficial transfer, and long-term outcome improvement remain
unverified.

`build_ordinary_chat_history_intervention_protocol` now creates a digest-bound
pre-registration object from that bundle. It binds the source session and turns,
prior response digest, serving-model identity, generation-configuration digest,
task commitment, independent scoring-plan digest, and seeded, balanced trial
index. Its verifier rechecks the prompt bundle and keeps authority, effect, and
learning claims false. `OrdinaryChatHistoryInterventionStore` publishes and
reloads this object immutably under the existing custody-checked experiment
protocol store. The governed chat adapter and condition runner now consume the
protocol, though no live experiment has used them.

The result-side contract is now available through
`build_ordinary_chat_history_intervention_result` and
`OrdinaryChatHistoryInterventionStore.persist_result/load_result`. A result
packet binds all three condition prompt digests to output digests, invocation
receipt references, and blinded score artifact references, and binds the packet
back to the exact protocol and prompt bundle. The packet is immutable in the
experiment run collection. The store reloads and verifies every referenced
score artifact's digest and output/plan binding. Result reload accepts the same
optional owner-backed receipt verifier used at persistence; without that
callback, receipt references are not independently reverified. Evaluation and
cohort persistence/reload forward this optional verifier, so a cohort can
recheck every invocation receipt when its verification owner is available. New
result persistence requires a receipt verifier and publishes one immutable
per-protocol finalization index bound to the execution claim and result digest;
a competing result packet cannot replace the finalized result. Finalization
also derives an immutable evaluation from
the reverified scores. It reports per-condition correct counts and the two
preregistered paired count deltas, retains within-suite-indexed per-probe outcomes,
keeps selective abstentions and invalid answers distinct, and marks unavailable
or truncated outputs unscoreable. Per-probe correctness deltas expose whether
aggregate changes came from broad shifts or a small subset of probes. Evaluation reload
recomputes the digest and score semantics from the stored plan and score
artifacts. These are descriptive outcomes for one preregistered task, not
evidence of effect, improvement, or learning.

`build_ordinary_chat_history_intervention_cohort_summary` combines verified
evaluation artifacts from one session, task, scorer, serving-model identity,
generation configuration, shared non-history prompt, and exact selected-history
projection. It requires a complete trial sequence from
index zero in full six-trial counterbalance blocks, so an interrupted or
omitted trial prevents a seemingly complete cohort summary. It verifies that
the six scheduled orders are distinct and each occurs once per block. It
reports paired correct-count deltas and paired per-probe outcomes for every
trial, aggregates
correctness and outcome transitions per probe across scoreable repetitions,
reports paired probe gains, losses, ties, and outcome-transition counts, and
reports restoration-score stability while retaining unscoreable outcomes in
the trial count. Each planned comparison is also stratified by the six exact
condition orders, with condition positions, paired score deltas, and per-probe
outcomes in each order, so position-dependent patterns stay visible instead of
being hidden in the pooled aggregate. It verifies paired probe identities and
probe-level deltas across trials and against aggregate deltas. This is a
descriptive within-task summary; it does not establish transfer, generalization,
improvement, or learning. Restoration reports aggregate score stability, exact
output-digest stability, and whether the same per-probe outcome pattern
returned as separate measures. The cohort rebuilds
restoration flags from paired condition metrics and rejects inconsistent score
or output-digest stability claims. Restoration stability is also stratified by
condition order, including per-probe outcome transitions. New summaries use
schema v6; v2 through v5 summaries remain verifiable in their original shapes.
`OrdinaryChatHistoryInterventionStore.persist_cohort_summary` reloads each
evaluation through its protocol, result, scoring plan, and score artifacts
before publishing the summary immutably. `load_cohort_summary` repeats those
checks and verifies the summary digest on reload.

`execute_ordinary_chat_history_intervention` now supplies the dormant
three-condition orchestration boundary. It follows the frozen randomized,
position-balanced
order, requires a separate receipt-verification owner to bind every receipt to
the exact prompt, output, serving identity, and generation configuration, then
passes outputs to a scorer under freshly generated opaque sample IDs and a
shuffled order. The runner rejects anything other than completed,
non-truncated, non-fallback inference and requires the scorer to echo the bound
scoring-plan digest, scorer identity digest, and blinded-scoring posture. Score
artifact IDs and digests are copied into the result packet; output text and
score values are not retained by the runner. The runner does not
instantiate a model, and no live run has been performed. Integrators must
provide owner-backed callbacks; a callback's presence alone is not proof, and
the persisted result verifier continues to distinguish internal linkage from
independent artifact verification.

Before the first inference call, the runner publishes one immutable,
nonce-bound execution claim for the protocol. A second execution attempt is
rejected even if the first process stopped before producing a result. Such a
trial is censored as interrupted; continuing requires a newly scheduled trial,
so an operator cannot silently rerun the same preregistration and retain only
the more favorable outputs. Result persistence also requires that execution
claim. This is a one-shot custody boundary, not crash-resume support.

Protocol v3 binds a distinct scorer identity digest and a cohort digest over
the session, task, scoring plan and scorer,
serving-model identity, generation configuration, shared prompt, and
selected-history projection. The result v2 packet binds each condition's
outputs and scores back to that protocol. The
`build_ordinary_chat_history_objective_scoring_plan` helper freezes an existing
deterministic affine prediction task and evaluator source identity. The scoring
plan's question must occur in all three matched prompts, and
`persist_protocol(..., scoring_plan=...)` stores the private oracle plan before
the intervention runner can start. `OrdinaryChatHistoryAffineObjectiveScorer`
receives the shuffled, opaque samples and reuses SentientOS's deterministic
integer oracle. It publishes immutable `ordinary-history-score-*` artifacts
with the protocol and plan digests, output digest, opaque sample ID, bounded
score payload, and scorer identity digest. Result persistence and reload
recompute each score against the stored task plan. This verifies score
semantics under the bound local evaluator source; the source digest is not a
cryptographically authenticated scorer identity. New score artifacts require
protocol v5; protocol v1 through v4 artifacts remain readable for audit. V5
can additionally bind a numerically passing semantic-history retrieval
evaluation, including its conditional source-independence qualification, exact
query-set and target identity, and an exact held-out task question; the store
revalidates that immutable Phase A evidence on protocol reload and execution.
The verified evaluation summary also returns the bound gate status and
qualification posture with the protocol digest that carries them, so callers
can display a conditional pass as conditional without reopening the protocol
artifact. This is provenance visibility, not an added evidence-independence
claim or a learning result.
After
evaluator source changes, prior plans and score artifacts remain loadable and
retain their digest bindings, but automatic
semantic revalidation reports false; new scoring refuses a source mismatch.

The intervention store allocates a durable sequential schedule per source
session, task commitment, scorer plan and identity, serving-model identity,
generation configuration, shared prompt, selected-history projection, and any
bound semantic-retrieval Phase A evidence. It
creates an initial matched prompt bundle to derive that cohort identity before
allocating a trial index.
`next_trial_index` exposes the next slot before final condition ordering;
protocol persistence claims that slot immutably and
rejects skipped indices and a different protocol reusing a slot. The runner
requires the matching sequential schedule claim before executing. Existing v1
and v2 protocols and v1 schedule claims remain readable for audit; only v3
protocols use model-and-context-scoped schedules.

`OrdinaryChatHistoryInterventionStore.prepare_intervention` now joins the
preparation path: it builds the deterministic affine task and private scorer
plan, requires that task's question to be the exact current message, reads the
next scheduled index, creates the verified history-present/withheld/restored
prompt bundle, and persists the scorer plan, protocol, and schedule claim. It
returns the frozen artifacts with `inference_started=false`; calling it does
not invoke a model.

`OrdinaryChatHistoryChatInferenceAdapter` now adapts the existing `ChatInference`
and `verify_stored_chat_invocation` interfaces. It binds calls to the protocol's
source session and user turn, adds protocol and condition lineage to each call,
checks the current model identity and exact prompt digest before each
generation, and validates the persisted
receipt's model identity, prompt and output digests, generation configuration,
completion status, truncation state, and fallback state. The receipt verifier
returns those digest fields as part of its verified summary. This is a code
integration only; it has not been called against a live model or serving
session.

Ordinary fallback turn lineage also records a bounded retrieval posture:
`selected`, `provider_returned_no_context`, `provider_unavailable`,
`provider_failed`, `context_validation_failed`, `prompt_budget_unavailable`,
`prompt_budget_rejected`, or `not_considered`. This distinguishes a verified
history exposure from common absence and omission paths during later audit. It
does not claim that the model used the selected context. The selected IDs,
digests, and posture are copied to the assistant turn and compared with the
receipt's caller lineage during idempotent recovery; legacy turns without this
lineage remain recoverable under their earlier contract. Provider failures
remain non-blocking for ordinary chat, and no raw prompt or exception text is
retained. When a history packet is selected, the exact prompt block's digest is
also saved on the assistant turn and checked through the receipt's caller
lineage. The response exposure has additive versions: v2 adds the prompt-block
digest, v3 the retrieval policy, v4 the retrieval target identity, v5 the
history-state digest, and v6 the actual serving-model identity digest. Older
lineages retain their earlier shape. The exposure reports
`influence_posture` as `not_measured`, so
the digest establishes what was presented, not whether the model used it. When
operator feedback cites a v2 exposure, its v7 feedback record preserves the
same prompt-block digest with the existing unverified-report posture. The digest
is an opaque content identity: consumers cannot reconstruct prompt text from it,
and it does not show attention or influence. The v7 reader also requires the
report's conversation citations to include the assistant turn and response
digest that the exposure describes.
The literal record-ID citation audit's v3 form also records the canonical
assistant-turn digest alongside the raw response-text SHA-256; v4 adds the
serving-model identity digest carried by the selected-history exposure. Saved
transcript diagnostics recompute this audit from the saved response and turn
linkage, and the response view displays these digests. The scan only
establishes whether exact record IDs appeared in the response text; it is not a
measure of attention, influence, accuracy, or learning.
When an operator supplies feedback about that response, the validated audit is
copied into the attributed feedback record under schema v12. The resident
feedback reader verifies that every cited record ID and digest belongs to the
same selected history packet before including the report in later historical
context, and binds the audit's response digest to the exact assistant response
cited by the feedback record. The audit remains an observation about response
wording, not proof of reliance or developmental improvement.
The resident cognition prompt explicitly tells later responses how to interpret
the serving and target identity digests and the citation audit, including that
absence of a literal citation is not evidence that history was unused.
The feedback-to-history path now accepts two distinct source postures. A
screen-bound report retains the cited response's source capture digest and a
fresh follow-up capture. A conversation-only report instead binds the source
user turn ID, the exact cited user and assistant turn digests, and session; its
absent source-capture digest is explicit in schema v9, while the follow-up
capture remains separate context. Both remain `operator_assertion_unverified`, claim no proven effect,
and grant no authority. The conversation-only source does not turn a user
correction into independently verified truth; it makes the reported
correction available as attributed experience for later cognition.

Ordinary-chat prompt guidance now distinguishes digest-verified record
integrity from the truth of claims inside a record. When a selected packet
contains nested operator feedback, ordinary chat is instructed to retain its
`operator_assertion_unverified` posture and describe it only as an operator
report. It may use the linked history-exposure metadata to understand what was
available to the earlier assistant turn, but that metadata is not evidence of
model reliance, report accuracy, or learning. Conversation citations in that
packet identify turns by digest; ordinary chat does not infer their text from
those identities and compares a report with prior dialogue only when the exact
cited text is present in its session history.

The companion bridge now forwards the exact serving-model identity to the
resident owner's model-aware ordinary-history projection. This lets the
existing retrieval use exact-identity relations where source records carry
model bindings, without treating model identity as proof of transfer or
correctness. Older owners that implement only the model-independent provider
remain usable through the legacy bridge method. The serving identity and
selected record references continue to be bound by ordinary-chat invocation
lineage; history exposure still does not show that the model attended to or
learned from the records. Selected ordinary-chat history now also records a
separate `serving_model_identity_digest` for the identity captured at the
invocation boundary. It is distinct from `target_model_identity_digest`, which
describes the identity supplied to model-aware retrieval. The v6 exposure
includes the serving digest, and receipt-backed recovery checks it against the
verified serving identity. This makes legacy model-independent retrieval
auditable across model replacement without implying that retrieval was
model-aware or that the selected history influenced the response. The
operator-feedback bridge preserves this field under feedback schema v11, and
the resident reader verifies its shape before projecting the report as
historical context. Older feedback versions remain readable under their prior
contracts.
When operator feedback carries this exposure, schema v10 preserves its
`target_model_identity_digest` through the bridge and the resident feedback
reader. The reader revalidates the digest's shape while retaining the report's
operator-assertion posture; it does not treat the identity as corroboration of
the reported content.
Ordinary-chat guidance also treats a task-family example's
`model_identity_relation` as source-model applicability metadata. Matching or
different identities do not establish that its performance transfers to the
current task or model. Material claims that rely on a developmental record now
cite its containing `record_id` and remain identified as historical claims.
Ordinary-chat response diagnostics and the local saved-transcript view include
a bounded literal `devrec-*` token scan against the selected record IDs,
pairing each selected-ID match with that record's digest. The v2 audit binds
the scan to the exact response text digest, history selection digest, prompt
block digest, and retrieval policy ID when available. It distinguishes
selected-ID, outside-selection, and no-explicit-citation cases and reports
whether the bounded scan completed. This measures citation-format compliance
and exposure provenance only; influence and learning remain unmeasured. The
display is a review aid, not a learning score or a proxy for model attention.

Ordinary-chat history selections also carry the explicit
`ordinary_chat_query_decision_polarity_then_lexical_overlap_with_identity_bound_correction_groups_and_embodied_reports_then_recency_fill:v1`
retrieval policy ID. The ID makes the deterministic ranking path auditable
instead of labeling it `owner_default`; it identifies how records were ranked,
not why the model used them or whether they helped.
The policy ID is now retained in the assistant turn's invocation lineage and
the v3 ordinary-history exposure. When a later operator feedback record cites
that exposure, schema v8 preserves the policy ID with the selected record and
prompt digests; legacy exposure and feedback schemas remain readable. This
strengthens selection provenance only and does not upgrade operator feedback
from an unverified report.

Resident prompt assembly selects at most four verified memory records. Omitting
additional records because of that context cap or the character budget is a
selection limitation, not incomplete source verification: the context reports
`matching_records_omitted` while retaining the underlying retrieval posture when
every inspected source passed. Actual rejected or budget-deferred sources still
produce a partial-verification retrieval posture. A receipt-backed CPU fixture
checks five verified records from one source session against the four-record
prompt cap.

When selected task-family history contains score-bound incorrect examples, the
live cognition prompt now explicitly identifies them as source-task records of past errors,
not mappings or answer keys. It directs the model to reason from the current
task and evaluator-correct examples, and to preserve uncertainty or abstain
when those sources do not support an answer. This clarifies how negative
experience can inform later reasoning without turning a failed output into
positive guidance. Before a governed request is admitted, the task-family
prompt projection also verifies that this exact instruction appears whenever
the selected packets contain prior incorrect examples; the projection binds the
same request prompt digest recorded by the invocation receipt. This verifies
delivery into the prompt, not model attention or learning. The invocation
request also carries an answer-free v1 evidence row binding the instruction
digest and prior-error count to the task-family projection and exact prompt
digest. The cognition owner reconstructs the row before inference, and the
governed invocation receipt binds it as request evidence.

Every companion-history intervention now reconciles its live condition captures
with the reloaded run's verified observations and decision packet before
returning a result. The owner checks observation and receipt identities, prompt
and generation controls, retrieved record IDs and digests, current-fact IDs,
response digest, disposition validation, and evidence/history citations. It
also validates the digest-bound matched-input packet against the question,
snapshot, current projection, and supplied prior self-model and epistemic state,
then requires that packet to match across all three conditions. This applies
equally to history-recall and action-transfer probes; callers cannot
qualify a live capture that is absent from or inconsistent with the persisted
experiment evidence. The check establishes provenance, not that the historical
decision was correct or that it caused an external effect.

Ordinary-chat history intervention verification now binds each persisted model
invocation receipt to the exact protocol condition as well as its prompt and
output digests. It checks the receipt's session and source-turn linkage and the
caller context inside the serving request linkage, including protocol ID,
protocol digest, and condition.
This prevents a valid receipt from another intervention condition from being
reused as evidence for the current one. It strengthens attribution only; it
does not establish a history effect or durable learning.

Task-family history selection now quarantines
`rootless_integer_affine_transfer_low_range_v5`. That family ID was reused by
rootless runs with different hidden affine mappings, so its examples cannot
safely support one persistent mapping or cross-run strategy. The immutable
records remain available to audit tools, but resident cognition returns no v5
examples for prompt projection and the candidate, rejected, and accumulated
strategy builders refuse that family ID. New rootless trials use the v6 family
with a stable hidden mapping and varying disjoint probes; the family ID check
keeps quarantined history out of those trials. If a required v5 history request
reaches the owner, it fails with
`task_family_history_quarantined_unstable_mapping_family` rather than the
generic empty-history error, preserving the distinction between quarantined
experience and retrieval failure. The read-only task-family history audit uses
schema v5 to report `task_family_contract_quarantined` and the bounded reason
code, separately from a family with no selected projectable history. Its
`audited_legacy_*` counts and source-run score projection are explicitly
audit-only; all projectable counts remain zero. This preserves retrospective
score binding without reopening the old records to prompts or strategy fitting.
The cognition projection helper has no quarantine-bypass option; only the
separate `_task_family_history_examples_for_audit` path can read those rows.
The v6 mapping and condition-specific probe ranges are enforced by the shared
`ObjectivePredictionTask` contract: acquisition tasks must use inputs 2–5 and
history-required transfer tasks must use inputs 6–9. A wrong multiplier,
offset, or condition/range pairing fails before the task plan is persisted.
Changing either range or the mapping requires a new family ID and explicit
migration or quarantine policy; changing the runner's constants while keeping
v6 would invalidate the family contract.

The v7 family `rootless_integer_affine_transfer_wide_range_v7` keeps the same
fixed mapping but registers acquisition inputs in `[2, 9]` and history-required
transfer inputs in `[10, 1000]`. Its seeded constructor is opt-in through a
v6 campaign plan; the no-argument family constructor continues to produce the
historical v6 tasks byte-for-byte for existing seed replay. The separate
ranges remove exact acquisition/transfer input reuse by construction and put
the transfer in an extrapolation range. They do not guarantee disjointness from
all accumulated v7 history across later cycles, so every seeded preflight and
completed transfer must still measure exact overlap against the bound history
and selected prompt records.
The rootless launch validator now performs a fail-closed overlap measurement
against all owner-projectable v7 examples in the frozen prior-history boundary
before model setup. It binds the result and the prior boundary digest into run
preregistration; incomplete measurement or exact reuse rejects launch. This
prevents a later campaign cycle from labelling a reused historical probe as
held out, while preserving the separate exact prompt/delivery audit required
after acquisition. The gate is v7-only and does not assert model attention,
causal use, or learning.
The campaign audit v8 independently recomputes overlap for completed runs from
the manifest-bound prior record bytes. An incomplete launch that did not copy
those records can retain only a plan-bound, self-digested preflight; its audit
marks overlap as unrecomputed rather than upgrading that claim.

For wide-range v7 campaigns, an optional plan-bound initial-history seed bundle
now lets the offline campaign audit recompute first-cycle overlap even when an
incomplete launch stopped before copying history into its run root. The v9
audit records the verified bundle digest and overlap-recomputation status.
Bundle creation copies developmental records and receipts into a private output
location; it establishes byte-level overlap evidence only, not model attention
or learning. If no bundle is supplied, the incomplete-cycle result remains
unrecomputed.

The prepared first-cycle v7 preflight's source history has since been recovered
from the ext4 mirror and reopened against its exact 46-file frozen boundary.
The v5 independent source audit reprojected 36 prior v6 examples (inputs 2–9)
and found no overlap with the planned v7 transfer inputs `[125, 178, 921]`.
It binds the family projection implementation digest and verifies that the
history boundary remained stable during the read. This upgrades the held-out
input-disjointness evidence for that frozen history; the campaign cycle has not
run, so model use, transfer performance, and learning remain unestablished.

The first campaign plan failed CPU identity replay and remained immutable. A
second wide-range v7 plan fixed the identity digest but omitted its recipe and
also remained unlaunched. The first recipe-bound schema-v7 plan then exposed
launch-gate omissions for runtime-admission and per-cycle identity bindings;
after those were fixed, the runner stopped before inference because Linux Git
could not read the Windows worktree pointer. A private Git-for-Windows wrapper
now produces complete source provenance from the WSL runner. A fresh plan,
`rootless-history-campaign-9cf3b1f3bff547c1820b`, includes the same exact
manifest, model tag, GPU layers, runtime/generation options, and normalized
identity implementation hashes. Its digest is
`944a645d8b7b387632851ce19fde9842dc8aa0e3c5307bb10b6b88df2db64a05`; its plan,
CPU owner/binder preflight, source-history audit, and private seed bundle are
documented under `artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cf3b1f3bff547c1820b`.
The owner selects no initial records; transfer probes `[66, 711, 820]` have
zero overlap with the initial history and all 16 alternate seed sets. The fresh plan reached launch preflight, where schema-v7 boundary, identity, history, held-out, and source-provenance checks passed. It stopped before model loading because the RTX 3060 had only 6,436 MiB free and 87% utilization; its cycle ID is now occupied by an incomplete failure artifact. The second planned cycle was not launched. These checks do not demonstrate model attention, durable learning, or transfer; learning remains unestablished.

Cross-run affine strategy accumulation now also resolves rootless family IDs
against that shared fixed-mapping registry. It rejects unregistered rootless
families, source examples outside the registered probe ranges, and a fitted
relation that differs from the family mapping, even if a subset of persisted
answers would fit some other affine rule. This keeps accumulated cognition
bound to the preregistered family contract instead of inferring the contract
from whichever examples happen to survive filtering. A deterministic anchor
pair must each contribute one evaluator-correct probe input absent from the
other member of the pair, with at least three distinct inputs in their union.
The selected pair maximizes union size, then combined unique-input count, with
sorted run digests breaking ties. Repeated probe inputs remain visible in the
overlap evidence; other source runs can corroborate the deduplicated examples
without changing the anchor pair. A run may contribute three or more distinct
probes: the gate is on support across multiple runs, not on limiting how many
valid examples one run can contain.

Interactive prompt selection now carries bounded coverage counts rather than
repeating every available and omitted record ID and digest in the model input.
Exact identities remain in the durable cognition selection and source packets;
selected records and citation allowlists still bind the history the model can
cite. A history-neutral prompt comparison removes these condition-dependent
counts along with treatment and record identities. This is context compaction,
not evidence of model attention or learning.

The 2026-11-22 rootless cycle advanced past the prior input-budget failure after
this compaction, then stopped while projecting a retained
`developmental_history_intervention_run:v3` whose digest-bound summary
classification agreed with its fact disposition but lacked the newer explicit
top-level classification bindings. The prompt projection now preserves such a
summary only as `legacy_summary_only` with
`legacy_summary_unavailable`; it does not infer the underlying structured
output or response-text decisions. Partially present or inconsistent bindings
remain fail-closed. This compatibility path was exercised by the first cycle
of the next prepared campaign (`rootless-cognition-cycle-d014bdb4dd2b3f9c8a71`).
That cycle completed with stable source provenance and verified copied
history. Its transfer probe recorded correct counts `[3, 0, 3]` for history
present, withheld, and restored, with restoration matching the present
condition. This is one synthetic, same-model task-family episode; it establishes
neither durable learning nor general performance improvement. The companion
history intervention rejected an invalid withheld-condition response, so its
outcome remains `invalid_condition_response_rejected`.

The campaign's second cycle persisted objective transfer artifacts, including
`devexp-run-01c201546e2fada86793595b`, whose recorded correct counts are
`[3, 0, 3]` and whose restoration matches the present condition. The parent
cycle nevertheless terminated at a later writeback tick with
`experiment_history_binding_drift`: the preregistered selection expected three
history records while the live selected set contained two. Since the cycle did
not publish a completed result and the run artifact has not passed semantic
loading with a receipt verifier, retain its score as **recorded but not
independently verified** evidence. Do not pool it with completed episodes or
make a learning claim.

The drift diagnostic now reports the durable selection summary and separate
prompt-selection counts, protocol selection digests, bounded prompt-size
attempts, and input lengths. It excludes prompt text and model output. This is
intended to locate whether the mismatch arose during selection, compaction, or
prompt construction without weakening history binding. The diagnostic was
exercised by a later cycle, documented below; the earlier campaign audit
correctly reported its own planned cycles incomplete and learning unestablished.

The objective history preflight now uses the same compact history packet modes
as objective inference. Previously preflight enabled compaction only for
companion interventions even though `_cognize` also enabled it for objective
tasks, which could size a different history suffix than the inference path.
The exact-set drift rejection remains in place; a fresh cycle must exercise the
alignment.

The first cycle after that change still failed during acquisition writeback.
Its diagnostic showed four available records, three admitted by protocol, two
selected by inference, and a 22,978-character prompt under the 24,576 limit.
Preflight had omitted the active-model identity that `_cognize` adds to the
actual context. Comparison ticks now capture that identity before retrieval,
include it in preflight sizing, and pass the same identity to every condition;
the existing probe-identity check rejects any change. The following campaign
completed without a history-selection mismatch.

Campaign `rootless-history-campaign-c1fdeb89b42fb7d4fd96` was prepared against
the most recent retained history boundary and verified before inference. Its
plan digest is
`4065d0960ce1b16caa45748db09711237aeb4b7659db73aacf229042ce6f833e`; the
append-only and seed-boundary audit digest is
`6bf43f8a1527217592e87dcac13de50abf524f1d0e64a10d309a9ebdde8af03e`. Both
planned cycles completed with stable source provenance and verified copied
history. The second cycle seeded from the first cycle's retained history. Each
objective transfer run passed controlled-observation validation, and its
history-present and restored conditions used the same selection digest; the
withheld condition selected zero records. This exercises the aligned preflight
and identity context without weakening exact history binding.

The first transfer scored `[3, 0, 3]` correct probes for present, withheld,
and restored conditions, with full answer coverage and stable restoration. The
second scored `[0, 0, 0]`, also with full answer coverage and stable
restoration. Both had zero abstentions. The first episode therefore showed a
history-associated correctness difference; the next did not reproduce it.
These are two synthetic, same-model task-family episodes, not evidence of
durable learning, general improvement, or a stable benefit from history. The
campaign audit reports `learning_established: false` and
`all_planned_cycles_completed: true`.

A subsequent fixed-mapping v6 campaign completed two more same-model cycles.
Its history-chain audit verified both cycles and append-only history; the v17
retrospective audits verified receipt and owner-run reloads. Cycle 1 transferred
at 3/3 with history present, 0/3 withheld, and 3/3 restored. Cycle 2 did not
qualify for transfer because its withheld baseline scored 1 against the
preregistered threshold of 2. This is one history-associated episode without
replication; model attention, durable learning, and stable improvement remain
unestablished. See the [campaign record](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#fixed-mapping-v6-replication-campaign)
and preserved [plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_4e092c83fada7a3dba01/plan.json),
[campaign audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_4e092c83fada7a3dba01/audit.json),
[cycle 1 audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_cognition_cycle_a02be6d3b0e82fe59391/retrospective_acquisition_history_audit_v17.json),
and [cycle 2 audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_cognition_cycle_baf4c2d89e35592a4c01/retrospective_acquisition_history_audit_v17.json).

The receipt-bound strategy projection produced no candidate from cycle 1's
available history, and cycle 2 had no transfer projection. The next check is to
verify that a frozen history boundary contains receipt-verified examples with
independent run anchors before allocating another transfer comparison; details
are recorded in the [projection follow-up](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#accumulated-strategy-projection-follow-up).

Cycles 1 and 2 each retained verified acquisition records with complementary
correct input sets: `[3, 4, 5]` and `[2, 4, 5]`. Both records and receipts are
present in cycle 2's history store. A two-cycle follow-up plan is prepared from
that boundary to inspect whether the resident projection jointly admits those
examples and binds a provisional candidate into the exact prompt. See the
[prepared plan and review gate](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#two-run-accumulated-history-follow-up-plan).
The plan has not been executed; candidate formation, transfer, and learning
remain unestablished.

The read-only task-family history audit now reports independent-input anchor
coverage by source run, including counts and digests for correct inputs and
whether two runs contribute distinct correct inputs across at least three
inputs. It excludes input and answer values. This makes evidence sufficiency
reviewable while keeping anchor availability distinct from a fitted strategy
or a learning claim.

The prepared follow-up boundary has now passed this offline inventory: all 23
record/receipt pairs verified, and 11 score-bound source runs contribute 8
distinct correct inputs with no conflicting correct answers. The audit does not
establish joint retrieval or exact-prompt delivery; that must be inspected in
the later cycle. See the preserved
[boundary audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/initial_history_anchor_audit_v3.json)
and [full review](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#offline-anchor-audit-of-the-prepared-boundary).

### Compact per-record strategy projection

The exact-prompt history projection now stores a compact, digest-bound summary
for each provisional affine strategy when input-budget compaction is enabled.
It keeps the fitted relation, supporting-example count and digest, source
fact/run/task/score bindings, model-identity relation, original strategy
digest, and explicit historical/non-authoritative claim flags. The projection
auditor re-derives the full strategy from the score-bound examples and verifies
that summary before accepting it. The accumulated strategy builder continues
to derive its result from examples across selected records; it does not treat a
per-record summary as independent evidence.

The compact packet also omits the verbose source model-identity map after its
canonical digest and applicability have been checked. It retains that digest,
binding posture, target identity digest, and an explicit omission marker. The
execution-condition list is represented by a count and a digest over the
ordered receipt bindings. Full examples, score digests, and durable record
bindings remain available to the projection and are not removed from stored
history.

This removes repeated derived fields from each compact record so the bounded
suffix selector can spend more of its prompt allowance on independent source
runs. The pre-change owner receipt selected 1 of 4 available records because
of the inference input budget. The compaction's effect on the selected suffix
and exact prompt still requires a later owner receipt; it does not establish
prompt delivery or learning by itself. The budget reprojection below is CPU
only; no Hermes inference was run for it.

An offline CPU reprojection of the exact four available records from owner
receipt `lmrec-61e79ae1e1777a744d728a10` measured compact packet sizes of 4,876,
4,876, 3,986, and 4,846 characters. The pre-change request used 20,270 of
24,576 input characters and selected one record. Using its 5,936-character
selected packet to estimate the unchanged remainder, the two-record newest
suffix projects to 23,166 characters (1,410 remaining); the three-record
suffix exceeds the bound. This is a CPU-only budget estimate, not an assembled
prompt or a new owner receipt. Both compact packet modes produced the same
sizes for these records. The preserved [reprojection artifact](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_reprojection_v1.json)
records source bindings, mode comparison, packet sizes, and these limitations.

The projected newest two-record suffix contains three score-bound incorrect
examples from `devrec-d476d487664a5dc051c71d75` and three correct examples from
`devrec-6e3f64dbeb9ae7ae911631c3`. The aggregate builder correctly produces no
fit from that suffix because only one run contributes correct examples. A
separate, non-suffix CPU diagnostic pairing `devrec-4fd52036e8b17c28ac02994c`
with the newest record yields a provisional relation from six distinct inputs
across two runs, while both source comparisons remain unavailable. The
aggregate schema v9 records that limitation, and its compact prompt summary is
re-derived from the selected packets by the projection auditor. That pair is
not admitted by the current recency-suffix policy; using the prior fixed-prompt
remainder, its compact summary still puts the estimate above the input bound.
No transfer, model attention, or learning claim follows from this diagnostic.
See the [expanded budget and evidence audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_reprojection_v2.json).

The read-only CPU reprojection is now reproducible with
[`audit_compact_history_packet_budget.py`](../../work/audit_compact_history_packet_budget.py).
Given the owner cycle directory as `<cycle>`, rerun it with the bound receipt
and the pre-change selected packet size:

```powershell
python work/audit_compact_history_packet_budget.py `
  --history-root <cycle>/resident-cognition/history `
  --owner-receipt <cycle>/inference-custody/receipts/lmrec-61e79ae1e1777a744d728a10.json `
  --previous-selected-packet-chars 5936 `
  --output artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_reprojection_v3.json
```

The new [reprojection report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_reprojection_v3.json)
revalidates all four available record bindings and reports packet sizes of
4,876, 4,876, 3,986, and 4,846 characters. Under the same fixed-remainder
estimate, the newest two-record suffix remains below the limit with 1,410
characters estimated spare; three records exceed it. Its two-record suffix
still contains correct examples from only one run, alongside the three
incorrect examples, so it does not support a multirun candidate. The report
explicitly records that it did not assemble the exact prompt, observe a new
owner receipt, run inference, establish learning, or confer authority. A fresh
governed owner receipt is required to measure the actual prompt after these
projection changes.

The subsequent Hermes transfer cycle and its receipt-bound prompt audit are
documented in the [GPU placement and cycle notes](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#prompt-audit-of-the-next-transfer-cycle).
That audit verifies a present/restored versus withheld projection contrast,
but finds only the newly acquired record in each exposed prompt and no
accumulated multi-run candidate. The score pattern is therefore one
history-associated observation; it does not show durable development. A
stale projection-version allowlist caused the cycle runner to stop after
inference. The validator now accepts the v32 evidence already emitted, and the
immutable [offline audit artifact](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/cycle_63d115b84ec6036b2bdd_prompt_audit_v1.json)
preserves the repaired validation without replaying inference.

A subsequent fresh Hermes cycle exposed a second validator assumption: present
and restored history may include older retained records alongside the latest
acquisition record. Its saved projection receipts verified equal
present/restored selections and an empty withheld condition; the old singleton
check rejected that valid selection shape. The runner now permits additional
records while requiring the acquisition record in both matching conditions.
The transfer scored 0/3 in every condition and delivered no accumulated
multi-run candidate, so this is a runner fix rather than evidence of transfer.
See the [answer-free audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/cycle_48dcc6e5c13a544f9b5a_prompt_audit_v1.json)
and [cycle notes](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#additional-retained-records-in-transfer-retrieval).

The follow-up CPU audit found the prompt-budget ranking then preferred an
exact-probe history row with no usable correct examples over source records
with score-verified positive inputs. Task-family retrieval now prioritizes the
number of distinct evaluator-correct inputs before exact-probe overlap, while
retaining the source outcome, model identity, and negative-history qualifiers.
A reprojection of the unchanged 13-record boundary puts two independent
correct runs in the simulated two-record prompt suffix, and the existing
candidate builder accepts a provisional six-input strategy from them. This
only demonstrates that qualifying evidence can be selected and fit; it does
not show exact prompt delivery, model attention, transfer, or learning. See
the [counterfactual audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/cycle_48dcc6e5c13a544f9b5a_retrieval_rank_reprojection_v1.json)
and [retrieval notes](rootless_hermes_developmental_cycle_gpu_placement.md#correct-example-aware-history-ranking).
The resident owner was also replayed directly on its persisted composition
state: its new four-record selection contained evaluator-correct coverage
across all four records and omitted the score-incomplete exact-overlap record. The
history boundary remained stable. This verifies selector behavior at the
owner projection stage; exact prompt budget fit still needs a governed receipt.
See the [direct owner replay](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/cycle_48dcc6e5c13a544f9b5a_owner_reprojection_v1.json).


### Follow-up: retrieval ranking meets the prompt budget

The latest direct owner replay confirms that the corrected ranking returns
four records with positive evaluator-correct coverage, but it did not assemble
the actual prompt. The existing compact-packet budget audit shows why that
distinction matters: its newest two-record suffix contains correct examples
from only one run, while a diagnostic pair of two positive runs produces a
old compact aggregate of 1,184 characters and an estimated total prompt of
25,240 characters against the 24,576-character bound, 664 characters over. The
compact aggregate now uses schema v2, omitting duplicate example and anchor
digests that are rederived from selected packets and still committed by the
full strategy digest. A representative serialization saves 259 characters;
applying that reduction leaves an estimated 405-character overage. The exact
source payload was not available for this size comparison, so this remains a
projection estimate. It uses the earlier fixed-prompt remainder and the pair
`devrec-4fd52036e8b17c28ac02994c` plus
`devrec-6e3f64dbeb9ae7ae911631c3`; it is not an exact prompt assembly and is
not the newest suffix from the direct owner replay. Therefore the recent
ranking change improves the candidate pool, but prompt-level delivery of the
multi-run candidate remains unverified and may still be blocked by both
suffix-only selection and input size. The next improvement should address
budget-aware evidence selection and further prompt reduction, followed by a
governed receipt check when GPU access is available. No new inference was performed for this
audit, and it establishes no model attention, transfer, or learning. See the
[budget follow-up audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_followup_audit_v1.json).


A CPU-only replay against the persisted cycle record store tightened this audit.
The exact saved receipt `lmrec-61e79ae1e1777a744d728a10` used a 20,270-character
prompt under a 24,576-character limit and selected only
`devrec-6e3f64dbeb9ae7ae911631c3`, despite four available records. Reprojecting
those four records with the current code measured compact packets of 4,862,
4,862, 3,981, and 4,860 characters. The positive pair
`devrec-4fd52036e8b17c28ac02994c` and
`devrec-6e3f64dbeb9ae7ae911631c3` forms a provisional six-input, two-run
candidate; its v2 compact summary is 856 characters. Using the prior fixed
prompt-remainder estimate gives 24,912 characters, 336 over budget. The shared
instruction text was shortened by 414 characters in this turn, producing a
nominal 78-character margin if that reduction applies to the saved prompt. The
prompt was not reconstructed, and the exact instruction baseline in that
receipt is unknown, so this still does not prove fit. See the updated
[budget follow-up audit](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/compact_packet_budget_followup_audit_v1.json).


The current-code replay also compacted each per-run strategy from 1,051 to 546
characters. Its parent history packet still carries the source identity and
score-bound examples, and the prompt validator rederives the full strategy
from those fields. The two positive packets now total 8,841 characters; adding
the v2 aggregate (856) and the prior fixed-remainder estimate (14,334) yields
24,031 characters, nominally 545 under the limit before the instruction-text
reduction. The current projection validator accepted both per-run v2 summaries
and the six-input/two-run aggregate during an offline CPU replay. The fixed
remainder is still an estimate and the pair is not confirmed as the actual
owner-selected suffix, so exact prompt fit and delivery remain unverified.


The current history binder then assembled the two source packets with the v2
aggregate into a 19,659-character prompt under the 24,576-character bound,
selecting both positive runs. This CPU replay used the persisted records and
target identity with the current instruction, but an empty current-evidence
placeholder; it is not an exact reconstruction of the historical cycle
prompt. The saved cycle receipt still shows that its then-current owner exposed
one record. A governed owner preflight must verify the new selection and full
prompt before inference. No inference or learning claim was made.

### Preflight of the planned follow-up cycle

The first cycle of the prepared two-cycle campaign was replayed against its
frozen initial history. The v8 audit builds the same synthetic pre-action body
observation shape as the runner, with a fixed-length timestamp placeholder,
and uses the resulting current-evidence payload. The owner ranked four
records; the binder selected two records for a provisional strategy supported
by two source runs and four distinct correct inputs. The prompt used 22,836
of 24,576 characters, including 2,019 characters of current evidence. Adding a
synthetic extra-evidence field preserved the candidate through 1,696 padding
characters (1,729 additional serialized evidence characters and 11 prompt
characters remained). At 1,712 padding characters (1,745 serialized evidence
characters), it selected one record and emitted no accumulated strategy; the
prompt then fell to 19,247 characters because one history record was omitted.
A 16-character-resolution sweep found no strategy reappearance through the
2,048-character point. The
v3 sensitivity is superseded: it appended padding to `snapshot_id`, a committed
identity field. The v4 audit leaves evidence identities intact and counts the
synthetic field's JSON overhead; v5 adds the finer transition sweep. V7 also
checks 16 alternate deterministic task seeds (2026112212–2026112227) against
the same frozen history. They cover all four transfer probe triplets from
inputs 6–9. All 16 retain a provisional strategy supported by two source runs
and four distinct inputs, at the same 22,836-character prompt length. The
binder selects two different record sets across those seeds. The relation is
`output = 2 * input + 1` in every row and the normalized candidate-core digest
is the same; the full evidence-bound strategy digest has two variants, tracking
the differing selected history lineage. This is post-hoc owner/context
sensitivity, not a preregistered model outcome. The planned descriptor's
nonce-derived commitment remains zeroed for repeatability. The model-generated acquisition
record is absent and the timestamp is a placeholder, so exact cycle
reconstruction and learning remain unestablished. See the [plan-bound
preflight report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/planned_cycle_c4e8743bad34018ac728_owner_preflight_v8.json)
and [replay script](../../work/replay_planned_cycle_owner_preflight.py).
Inference and learning remain unestablished.

The v9 CPU audit adds, for each alternate seed, the projected task-family
probe inputs present in each owner-selected history record and their
intersection with that seed's transfer probes. This is descriptive exposure
evidence only: it does not reconstruct all owner-ranking terms or show that
overlap caused a record to rank higher. The frozen plan and seed are unchanged;
the audit still performs no inference and establishes no learning. See the
[v9 report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/planned_cycle_c4e8743bad34018ac728_owner_preflight_v9.json).

The v10 diagnostic trace exposes the owner's actual comparator values for each
matching family record: outcome priority, evaluator-correct distinct-input

requested. It does not enter the cognition prompt or affect selection.
The CPU preflight verifies the trace's sort order and selected-record set
against the owner projection for the planned seed and all 16 sensitivity seeds.
Across the 16 sensitivity seeds, the binder's two retained records match the
owner's top two in every row. The owner probe-overlap term ties at 3 for all 12
eligible records in every seed, so it does not discriminate within this
cohort. The observed top-two usable-input pairs are `[2, 2]` and `[3, 2]`;
when those terms tie, checkpoint order completes the ranking. This explains
the observed seed-sensitive binder sets for this retained history, without
showing that the underlying records improve model performance.
These ranking terms describe retrieval behavior; they do not establish
learning or validate the historical outcomes. See the
[v10 report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless_history_campaign_9cc30cb351fa44ad9d51/planned_cycle_c4e8743bad34018ac728_owner_preflight_v10.json).

### Observer receipt retrieval posture

Ordinary developmental-history retrieval now distinguishes observer receipt
provenance from environmental independence. A receipt marked
`same_process_synthetic_fixture` is described as a same-process fixture, does
not receive the independent-observer relevance bonus, and remains historical
provenance only. A receipt without that fixture marker is described as an
observer source claimed distinct at ingress; retrieval explicitly records that
independence is not reverified on load. Neither posture proves a physical
consequence or that a model relied on the retrieved report.

To inspect the model-independence boundary without occupying the GPU, I
replayed the same frozen wide-range transfer history against a Qwen 3 8B
target identity. The history boundary and task-generation/backend seeds match

manifest digest `ebb2efeea46036ca7f276589e710729aff06c3a4b7b7b970c09da1712daf0989`,
while the Qwen target is pinned to manifest digest
`500a1f067a9f782620b40bee6f7b0c89e17ae61f686b92c24933e4ca4b2b8b41`; the
owner therefore evaluates these examples under a different model identity.

Both Qwen-target cycle preflights select the same two source records and
reconstruct a provisional strategy from two score-bound runs and six distinct
correct inputs. All 16 alternate task seeds retain it, and the planned probes
remain disjoint from selected history. The plan digest is
`8de9a34f61362aae30b0ff609d9ec9901aeb8a4457f46e3ad1a58d2e83229593`.
See the [plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-310583d7fb52477180e7/plan_v1.json),
