### Strategy-inclusion owner-preflight evidence (CPU-only)

The CPU owner replay now builds a paired prompt audit that removes only the
top-level accumulated candidate strategy while preserving the exact selected
history packets, record IDs and digests, current evidence, and per-record
candidate strategies. On both target model artifacts and both planned cycles,
the accumulated strategy is present in the baseline prompt and its removal
reduces the prompt by 1,097 characters. The selected history packet digest is
unchanged. This establishes that the derived multi-run candidate changes the
prepared input independently of record retrieval; it does not establish that a
model attends to the candidate, improves on held-out probes, or learns. The
nested report contract is defined in the
[strategy-inclusion ablation schema](rootless_planned_cycle_strategy_inclusion_ablation.schema.json)
and validated before the owner report is written.

The ablation contract and its schema are included in the current pin. The
refreshed [Hermes plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-f0ba12e3ab4fce872a11/plan_v3.json)
has digest `01a85fce313687712a876171b2953420aa7df68e3bab3cb6acf6f505d983f34c`;

has digest `db6c9bbe6266c56d68dae5f240fbe31cbe93bef4df7c18323f7afa21d7bd3cb2`.
Both verify against current implementation digest
`3975c835bd8128eba4f8f8f726177bdbb9235c3dfc41a58a00fbd3c826b4a27a`,
across 1,202 source and schema files, and their model identity recipes verify.
Their v16 CPU reports are [Hermes cycle 0](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-f0ba12e3ab4fce872a11/owner_preflight_cycle0_v18.json),

identity, history boundary, current implementation and model-identity pins, and
the nested schema. It reads report and schema files through bounded,

digests it verified. The plans and reports are CPU-only and retain
`inference_performed: false` and
`learning_established: false`. Cycle 1 remains an initial-state replay until a
completed cycle 0 supplies its actual history. The Qwen preflights classify both

classify the source artifacts as the same underlying artifact, despite distinct

cross-model behavioral transfer: neither target model was invoked.
#### Behavioral follow-up: preregister the paired strategy contrast

The prompt audit is a candidate intervention, not a behavioral result. The
next strategy-specific experiment should compare two prompts for the same
target-model identity and task instance: (A) the exact governed prompt with the
accumulated candidate strategy, and (B) that prompt with only the top-level
accumulated strategy removed. Both arms must retain byte-equivalent current
evidence, selected history packets, per-record candidate strategies, task
instructions, and held-out probe set. A report with
`exact_cycle_prompt_reconstructed: false` is insufficient to launch this
comparison; first reconstruct the actual cycle prompt after its acquisition
record and timestamp exist, then bind both arms to that prompt digest.

Before inference, freeze the target artifact/configuration identity, generation
settings, task and hidden answer key, probe set, arm order, and scoring version.
Use the deterministic task-family evaluator to score exact held-out answers;
record abstentions separately and score confidence with the existing Brier
metric when the response supplies a scorable confidence. Keep each arm's
response out of the other's context and do not write either response into
developmental history until both arms have completed and been scored. Score
without revealing the answer key or arm label to the response evaluator. The
primary contrast is the paired difference in exact held-out probe correctness;
report per-probe pairs and counts, not only a pooled mean. If the two arms differ
in any bound input other than the accumulated strategy, reject the pair as
unmatched. If either arm lacks a scorable response, retain that as missing or
abstention evidence rather than imputing correctness.

This contrast estimates the incremental effect of supplying the accumulated
strategy on that pinned task and model. It does not by itself establish durable
learning, generalization, or model-independent continuity. A separate
history-present versus history-withheld comparison is needed to estimate the
contribution of historical examples; it must not be folded into this ablation,
which intentionally preserves those examples. The current CPU reports do not
register a behavioral comparison, and this design note authorizes no inference.

#### Owner implementation boundary

The current `run_objective_intervention_once` path preregisters the fixed
The existing `history_present`, `history_withheld`, and `history_restored`
conditions remain a separate intervention. `history_withheld` removes the
developmental-history packets before prompt construction, so it cannot stand
in for the strategy-omitted arm. Reusing that condition would confound two
interventions and make a strategy-effect claim invalid. The strategy mode
described below retains the selected history and per-record evidence while
removing only the accumulated candidate. Its resident owner path is implemented
but has not been run; matching invocation receipts and paired behavioral scores
are still required before interpreting it as evidence.

An initial CPU-only protocol/scorer primitive is now in
[`work/strategy_inclusion_intervention.py`](../../work/strategy_inclusion_intervention.py).
It prepares its protocol directly from the resident's owner-composed prompt
pair, taking exact context, prompt, preview-request, history, and ordered probe
bindings from that preview instead of requiring the caller to reconstruct the
baseline context. It binds those fields to model identity, task, and generation
settings.
Its scorer
accepts paired answers against one hidden deterministic key and reports
per-probe correctness and optional integer Brier scores without returning the
key or predictions.
`score_verified_strategy_inclusion_pair` additionally requires one validated
local-model invocation receipt per arm, checks each request's actual prompt
digest, task, model identity, and generation configuration against the pair,
and scores answers parsed from the receipt-bound output text. Its output carries
both receipt digests and marks paired delivery as verified. Preparation now
requires the resident's owner-composed prompt-pair preview and binds its exact
per-arm prompt digests and preview request digests alongside the shared task,
history, model identity, and held-out probe bindings. The resident's request
digests are retained as preview provenance; the scorer does not require them to
match live receipt digests because execution uses its own tick and protocol
linkage. The receipt scorer
checks each live request's exact prompt digest, task, model identity, generation
configuration, and output schema before scoring its bound output. It maps
ordered answer and confidence arrays onto the preregistered probe digests and retains
abstentions as outcomes and reports coverage separately from correctness. The
governed output schema allows nullable answers for v4-v6 family tasks and
requires aligned confidence values for v5/v6 tasks. This establishes which
requests were delivered and that the owner constructed the matched prompt
pair; it does not establish durable learning.
The refreshed rootless replacement campaign plans and owner preflights bind
the then-current source pin. Any later edits to pinned source require another
refresh before those reports can serve as current preflight evidence. No model
inference was performed for this prompt-preview change.

The resident now exposes `preview_objective_strategy_inclusion_pair`, a
CPU-only owner path that builds both governed requests from the same live task,
snapshot, selected history, and model identity before either arm invokes the
model. It checks the serialized prompts directly and accepts the pair only
when the included and omitted contexts differ in the single top-level
accumulated-strategy field. Its result binds each exact prompt digest and
request digest, plus the shared task, history, snapshot, generation budget,
and model identity. Raw prompts stay inside the owner call and are not included
in the returned pair artifact. Preview protocol and objective-task-plan payloads
are built in memory; the dry run publishes no experiment protocol, task-plan
artifact, or incomplete cognition tick, and does not advance the order-trial
ledger. The request capture occurs after request construction and owner binding
checks, before invocation or resource-context consumption.

This closes the task-commitment gap in CPU preflight. The new
`execute_and_persist_strategy_inclusion_pair` coordinator freezes the protocol
in the experiment store before calling the owner-supplied arm executor. It
executes the preregistered arm order sequentially, validates both returned
receipts, scores their outputs against the held-out key, then persists the
answer-free paired score. The callback receives one arm and the frozen
protocol, not the other arm's output. No model call was made while adding this
path.

Before each callback, the store publishes one exclusive immutable arm claim;
after receipt verification, it publishes that arm's receipt identity binding.
A repeated call skips arms with completed bindings and can reload their receipts
through a caller-supplied receipt loader. For a claim without a completion
binding, an optional read-only recovery callback may return candidate persisted
receipts. The coordinator validates every candidate against the frozen arm
prompt, task, model identity, generation configuration, output contract, and
receipt integrity, considers at most 256 candidates, and binds only when
exactly one candidate verifies. It
never invokes a model during recovery. Missing, invalid, or ambiguous matches
still fail closed and require manual receipt reconciliation; the coordinator
never replays that arm automatically. Even a verified paired score establishes
behavior only on its pinned task and model, not durable learning or
generalization.

`ResidentCognitiveInvocationReceiptReader.find_persisted_invocation_receipt_candidates`
now provides the bounded read-only discovery path over resident inference
receipt custody. It re-reads each candidate, validates its receipt and serving
session linkage, and only then applies the caller's predicate. It compares the
bounded receipt-name inventory before and after the scan and rejects recovery
if that inventory changes during discovery.
`build_strategy_inclusion_receipt_recovery_callback` adapts that reader to the
pair coordinator and filters candidates against the exact frozen arm binding.
This closes receipt discovery for the resident-serving path without granting
the reader model or invocation capabilities.

The existing custody-aware developmental experiment store now accepts these
pair protocols as immutable protocol artifacts and accepts a paired score only
after recomputing it from the supplied answer key and both supplied invocation
receipts. The score artifact keeps the answer-key digest, paired outcomes, and
receipt bindings; it does not store the answer key or model answer values.
Reload verifies the artifact digest, protocol link, and each arm's persisted
receipt binding. Full invocation receipts remain in governed invoker custody;
recovery therefore needs a receipt loader and, for a claim interrupted before
its result binding was written, the read-only resident receipt discovery
adapter. The pair store itself does not copy or independently authenticate
those source receipts. This does not
establish durable learning.

The resident objective owner now also accepts an explicit
`accumulated_strategy_inclusion` mode (`included` or `omitted`) on
`run_objective_intervention_once`. The mode is bound into the persisted
protocol's planned comparisons. In the omitted mode, history selection and
per-record evidence remain present, while only the aggregate strategy is
removed before prompt serialization. The task-family prompt projection uses a
separate v34/v35 posture and the owner rejects the arm if no derived strategy
exists to omit. The ordinary three history conditions and their scoring remain
unchanged. The owner still runs one mode per call; the coordinator joins those
calls only when the supplied receipts satisfy the frozen pair. The changed
owner, scorer, and campaign-pin code supersedes earlier frozen plan digests.

There is still no direct runner integration for the paired strategy
coordinator. `run_objective_intervention_once` runs the existing three history
conditions for each strategy mode, while the pair protocol and score bind one
receipt per arm. Calling that method from each arm callback would therefore
produce six invocations, of which four are outside the paired score. Do not
wire it directly as the two-arm executor. Before a behavioral run, either add
a governed single-invocation owner operation for the matched `history_present`
condition, or expand the preregistration and score to bind and interpret every
receipt produced by both three-condition runs. The CPU preflights above do not
resolve this execution-shape gap and authorize no inference.

### Single-invocation strategy arms (2026-11-23)

The strategy-inclusion owner now creates a dedicated v14 arm protocol whose
condition order contains only `history_present` and whose planned comparison
names exactly one arm. Both the CPU prompt preview and the executable arm use
this protocol, so the plan digest and request can be reconstructed identically.
The arm stores its task plan before inference, persists the resulting cognition
observation and completed-tick binding, and leaves paired scoring to the
strategy coordinator using the governed invocation receipt. It does not write
a fabricated three-condition run or claim learning. Non-strategy objective
interventions retain the existing three-history-condition protocol.

The prompt-pair preview and paired-intervention protocol are now v2 schemas.
Each owner preview carries an arm-specific execution tick ID, which the
frozen strategy protocol retains. `run_objective_intervention_once` requires
that protocol and the matching tick ID for a strategy arm. The owner compares
the newly constructed full request digest with the frozen preview digest before
calling the invoker; a changed prompt, linkage, or upstream binding therefore
fails before inference. `build_resident_strategy_inclusion_arm_executor` now
adapts the owner to the paired coordinator: it passes the frozen protocol and
tick ID, reopens the observation, and returns only the owner-verified persisted
invocation receipt. Request IDs and objective request digests remain explicit
per-arm inputs. No behavioral run has been performed here.

The strategy coordinator now independently checks each embedded owner protocol
before freezing the pair: schema v14, one `history_present` condition, exactly
the matching planned arm, the shared objective task digest, a valid protocol
digest and ID, a task-plan digest, and a distinct arm execution tick. The owner
preview includes the complete protocol so these bindings can be checked rather
than trusting only the preview's outer digest. The request digest is
reconstructible from stable semantic fields (including prompt, model identity,
and correlation ID); it contains no wall-clock timestamp. This closes a
protocol-validation gap but is not yet the separate live-owner CPU preflight
described below. No inference or behavioral result is implied.

This changes pinned cognition and protocol source. Earlier v5 and v6 campaign
plans and their owner reports predate these changes and must be regenerated
before any future model inference. No tests or model inference were run during
this implementation; only Python AST parsing was used to check syntax.

### Fresh Hermes campaign and runtime refusal (2026-11-23)

The WSL runner could not fingerprint this Windows linked worktree with Linux
Git, and its 32 MiB fingerprint bound was below the checkout's 54.4 MiB of
untracked source and evidence files. The runner now accepts
`SENTIENTOS_SOURCE_PROVENANCE_GIT` and retains the complete fingerprint under
a 64 MiB bound. With native Windows Git configured, source provenance is
`observed` and `worktree_content_status: complete`; no paths are omitted.

A fresh campaign
[plan v1](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-8aee6d5feda2453d9855/plan_v1.json)
has digest
`fb1ddde3fcd6fe7227f11816cdca252757a168ff4d27bd698eca924e3da167f9` and
verifies against 1,206 current implementation sources with both model
identity recipes verified. Its cycle-0 owner preview
([report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-8aee6d5feda2453d9855/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json))
has digest
`sha256:1a21d927a46760c21316fb347fe49e7ec50df763328b3af79c65a5ef8b917e60`,
passes independent verification, and retains three disjoint held-out probes.

The launch created an isolated run root and verified source provenance, then
stopped at `gpu_memory_headroom_insufficient` before model load or inference.
The host snapshot showed 5,770 MiB free, 81% utilization, and 63.5 C; the
runner requires the 9,001,755,690-byte model artifact plus a 2 GiB runtime
reserve, with a separate utilization ceiling of 25%. The cycle remains
incomplete with no behavioral result. Its write-once run checkpoint consumes
that cycle ID, so the next launch needs a fresh plan version, cycle ID, and
owner preflight after the runtime's GPU gates can pass.

The prior transfer failure's generic lineage error did not identify which
binding failed. Resident history writeback now attaches a bounded structural
diagnostic with one or more categorical checks (`lineage_field_shape_invalid`,
`score_schema_unsupported`, `evaluator_unrecognized`, `task_digest_mismatch`,
`output_digest_mismatch`, `lineage_digest_format_invalid`, or
`output_digest_format_invalid`). It retains no score values, answers, prompts,
or model output. This is an observability improvement, not a repair to the old
run's evidence. The shape is specified in
[`developmental_history_task_score_lineage_failure.schema.json`](developmental_history_task_score_lineage_failure.schema.json)
and is now part of the campaign implementation pin. The plan above pins the
prior writeback and campaign-builder sources and is stale after these changes;
prepare and independently verify a fresh plan and owner preflight before any
later model call.

after its seed history grew from 46 to 50 files. I regenerated [plan v2](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-be667728b2c041fdbfc2/plan_v2.json)
against the current history boundary (`a0ad094c0f83595e9d4cdb0ff5542dffaf9ed2e4d74a83a388af68ead34f9d33`).
Its plan digest is

1,207-source implementation pin independently verifies at digest
`0c4bbad57ee9bd4a224f3cff97ecccb2771e914da8531e49d229dfd26c3fecd6`.
The regenerated [cycle-0 owner preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-be667728b2c041fdbfc2/owner_preflight_cycle0_v2.json)
independently verifies with report digest
`sha256:b613532d81d5bc73f2f7c9413ca6f05b9a40254e5cfef2021f2c2702e4670940`.
It reserves three disjoint transfer probes, with zero exact historical-probe
overlap, and reconstructs both ablations. The projected prompt is 23,514
characters, within its bound. Inference and learning remain false; the new
cycle is prepared but unlaunched.

The failed historical cycle's score checkpoints show v5/v6 objective scores
with matching task and condition output digests, while its failure artifact
has no check-level diagnostic. A read-only reconstruction of both archived
score rows (acquisition v5 and transfer v6) passed all seven checks now
reported by the writeback validator: required-field shape, supported schema,
evaluator, task binding, output binding, lineage digest format, and output
digest format. This validates the stored score rows, but does not recover the
transient projected payload that failed at runtime or prove which older code
version rejected it. A wrapped writeback exception could also hide
the bounded diagnostic from the run-level failure writer. That writer now
walks at most eight causes and contexts, accepts only the known lineage
diagnostic schema and categorical check set, and reserializes only those
categories. It still excludes exception text, prompts, answers, and model
output. This gives the next failed transfer a more actionable artifact; it
does not retroactively identify the historical failure or repair its result.

The custody directory does retain both scored runs from that incomplete
cycle. I reconstructed their score digests and condition-output bindings
without retaining raw outputs in the audit artifact. The v5 acquisition run
scored 3/3 correct in all three conditions, including history withheld. Its
probe set is disjoint from the later transfer set. The v6 transfer run scored
0/3 correct with all three probes abstained in history-present,
history-withheld, and history-restored. Present and restored retrieved the
acquisition record; withheld retrieved none. This is a durable partial
behavioral result and negative evidence for transfer in this one episode, not
evidence of developmental learning. The cycle remains incomplete because the
subsequent history writeback failed. The exact failure revision is recorded as
`64844c0c49be1d972591d3fb39d52474345ad075`. At that revision,
`sentientos/resident_developmental_writeback.py` accepted task-score schemas
v3, v4, and v5 in the lineage validator, then added
`score_schema_unsupported` for any other version before raising
`developmental_history_task_score_lineage_invalid`. The transfer audit binds
the failed run's history-present score to schema v6 and verifies its task and
condition-output bindings. The source file at the pinned revision is Git blob
`4d1e3381f2776d11dec4178526008387eb9ef0c4`; its SHA-256 is
`5492c2fb02b20e30b8698beb87d0590d9ab7ec88601298e52f20f36729fa3611`.
Therefore, the old validator's v6 rejection is confirmed as a cause of the
writeback failure. Because the historical failure artifact omitted the
validator's `failed_checks`, this does not establish that schema rejection was
the only failing check. The current validator includes v6 in its accepted
lineage schemas; the old cycle remains incomplete and is not retroactively

## Semantic-index cache outcome is now explicit (2026-11-23)

Ordinary-chat semantic retrieval now reports `semantic_index_status` alongside
the digest of reusable cached entries. The status distinguishes a readable
empty index, a readable index with entries but no reusable vectors, an index
that supplied reusable vectors, an unavailable/custody-failed read, and a
retrieval where index loading was not attempted. The empty/nonempty distinction
uses the bounded scan's observed file count; it does not imply every nonempty
entry passed validation. This status does not change ranking: a failed derived-cache read
still leaves the exact current-query embedding batch available. Comparison
report schema v6 verifies the bounded status vocabulary, and cohort schema v13
records the sorted set of statuses observed across its questions and verifies
that set against the underlying reports. Older report and cohort schemas
remain readable under their original field contracts.

`readable_entries_without_reusable_vectors` remains broad: nonempty scans can
contain entries that failed validation, were stale or mismatched, or were
excluded by the current-tick rule. The snapshot digest continues to cover only
cached entries that contributed to ranking. No inference or learning claim
follows from adding this operational status. The entry-count refinement below
has its own updated matched-target source pin.

I refreshed the matched Hermes and Qwen preparation artifacts after this
implementation change. The Hermes [plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-465871c0317396c16f7a-hermes/plan_v1.json)
has digest `51f4548594d20451892f8a68cf189a45a5cddd2e54ee4aca10fb5cdfce7e9122`;
the Qwen [plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-465871c0317396c16f7a-qwen/plan_v1.json)
has digest `5295aabb0336a83db917518679d0e7324680a13deed5c215137f138a1eec5344`.
Both verify against implementation digest
`8364c356a5d96e559422fe6f2a6ffb0f803abb1aebdbd0dad5194bbc59ff2628` and the
same 46-file history boundary. All four CPU-only owner preflights independently
verify under report schema v18; cycle 0 has the same selected records and held
out probes `[11, 978, 991]` for both targets. The [matched-target record](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-465871c0317396c16f7a-qwen/matched_model_target_pair_v1.json)
verifies at `sha256:70ace397da1a3fd025aab84be7d1d9eb5f25427bca0b4465c7dfb31942807f65`.
These are preparation checks only: no model was loaded, inference was not
performed, learning and transfer remain unestablished, and cycle 1 must be
rebuilt after cycle 0 changes history.

The observed-entry count was then added to the stable semantic-index snapshot
so a readable zero-file index reports `readable_empty`, separately from a
nonempty scan with no reusable vectors. I regenerated the matched plans and all
four owner preflights against implementation digest
`bc4114662071f0d012cf0cd2aab601f3f9378c7d5184ab05e4b8d942b6073a0a`. Both
plan verifiers and all four preflight verifiers pass; the independent
[matched-target record](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-a78113d189d84ea6b52f-qwen/matched_model_target_pair_v1.json)
verifies at `sha256:a6d7389fc52dee12d9731a255cbc38f49554a12d95d0add5bb9655f9f2a114ba`.
The shared history boundary and cycle-0 probes remain unchanged. These remain
CPU preparation checks only; no model was loaded or inference performed.

## Semantic-cache exclusions are now distinguishable (2026-11-23)

Ordinary-chat retrieval now reports why a readable semantic cache supplied no
reusable vectors: every entry was invalid, no entry matched the available
history, cached record content was stale, all matching vectors came from the
current tick and were therefore excluded, or the vector dimension no longer
matched. Mixed or otherwise unclassified exclusions retain the prior broad
status. The index computes these categories from the same stable bounded
snapshot used for ranking; they do not alter ranking or fallback behavior.
Comparison report schema v7 accepts the refined status set, and existing cohort
schema v13 binds the observed sorted statuses through its underlying reports.
Older comparison reports remain valid under their original status vocabulary.
This makes future retrieval experiments able to separate index-custody/cache
problems from current-tick policy and history-version mismatches. It supplies
operational evidence only: no inference, retrieval-quality result, or learning
claim follows from a cache status.

In the configured WSL state root, a read-only scan found no persisted
`semantic_history_index_*` directories or comparison artifacts to exercise the
new classifications. The implementation has static Python compilation
evidence only; the new reason branches still need a real ordinary-chat
retrieval artifact before their runtime behavior is considered demonstrated.

The [refreshed Hermes plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-eb7864354ba05c7c6721-hermes/plan_v1.json)
and [Qwen plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-eb7864354ba05c7c6721-qwen/plan_v1.json)
supersede the prior matched plans because the retrieval report contract is

`4864221997da62c1a3183c82e514ab37b66747335e0ee78d7fc6e97ad6b4113a` and

## WSL source provenance with Windows linked worktrees (2026-11-23)

The WSL checkout is a Windows linked worktree whose `.git` pointer contains a
Windows path. Linux Git cannot resolve that pointer, so the bounded Hermes
runner records source provenance as unavailable unless
`SENTIENTOS_SOURCE_PROVENANCE_GIT` points to Windows Git. WSL interop supports
`/mnt/c/Program Files/Git/cmd/git.exe`; the runner then obtains the current
revision and worktree status while retaining only digests.

This worktree currently retains more than 64 MiB of untracked experiment
artifacts, while the tracked diff is about 2 MiB. The source fingerprint's
64 MiB bound therefore prevented complete worktree provenance even after Git
became available. I raised that bound to 128 MiB so the fingerprint covers the
full retained tree. This preserves the rule that inference requires a complete
source fingerprint; it does not omit artifacts or imply model learning.

Two launch attempts stopped before model loading and are retained as
incomplete, one-shot cycles. Cycle `rootless-cognition-cycle-260907ae1b8155b8de17`
stopped because the base WSL Python lacked `psutil`; cycle
`rootless-cognition-cycle-7e3b98d4a024ff19c68f` stopped because source
provenance was unavailable/incomplete. Neither attempt performed inference.
The dependency is now present in the isolated WSL experiment environment. A
fresh plan and cycle ID are required after this source-bound change, and the
next launch must set `SENTIENTOS_SOURCE_PROVENANCE_GIT` to Windows Git.

## Sequential Hermes history results (2026-11-23)

After adding the missing runtime dependency and making the full WSL source
fingerprint possible, two Hermes runs completed with stable source provenance
and governed unload. The first run used plan digest
`59cabac4ecb18901a302afc62471dbd1cc1d8f32f3d78bc96cbb0e5ca3852933`, seed
`13688478`, and cycle
`rootless-cognition-cycle-f7b0d61539a541b8a320`. Acquisition scored 0/3 in
history-present, history-withheld, and history-restored conditions, so the
held-out transfer task was withheld by the baseline-competence gate. It wrote
two records to its isolated history store. The synthetic embodiment fixture
reported `idle` after a `reach` prediction; the consequence was classified as
contradicted, with no physical action effect established.

I rebuilt the next plan against that completed history boundary. Its digest is
`52806ee5f0bb062c44f44b5a7bc72321965330b75be4e518b2a65d2b562ef34e`; the
cycle-1 owner preflight verified against the new boundary and reserved disjoint
probes `[23, 394, 499]`. Cycle
`rootless-cognition-cycle-622e5f90ab23477080cd` then copied 25 history records
and 25 receipts. On its same-seed acquisition comparison, the oracle scored
history-present 3/3, withheld 2/3, and restored 3/3. The outcome was
`history_associated_acquisition_gain_restored`: one extra correct probe with
history, with the score returning on restoration. The run’s own gates still
report that durable learning and performance improvement are not established.

The exact cycle-0 records were not selected by the cycle-1 owner preflight; it
selected two older records. The two runs also used different task-generation
seeds. Therefore the cycle-1 result is a bounded, same-run history-presence
association, not evidence that cycle 0’s specific experience taught the model
or that between-cycle performance improved. The cycle-1 transfer task recorded
zero correct held-out probes; two probes were unanswerable in history-present
and history-withheld conditions. Its new-action history gate also rejected the
same-process synthetic observer provenance. Separately, the companion
history-response comparison rejected the withheld output because it cited a
history ID disallowed in that condition, so that response comparison does not
qualify as a valid difference.

The compact, digest-bound [outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-eb7864354ba05c7c6721-hermes/hermes_sequential_history_observation_summary_v1.json)
binds both plans, owner preflights, cycle-result hashes, history boundaries,
task scores, intervention conditions, and limitations. Both runs recorded
complete, stable source provenance, `learning_established: false`, no
authority, and `governed_release_completed`.

The next useful experiment should make the exact retained record under study
survive owner selection and appear in the history-present prompt, then compare
it with withheld and restored conditions using the same task seed. For new
action decisions, use evidence with independently verified observation
provenance. The current two-cycle result is evidence that the owner can carry
history across isolated runs and that task scoring responds to history
presence in one controlled acquisition; it is not yet evidence of durable
development attributable to the preceding cycle.

## Interpreting invalid withheld-history responses (2026-11-23)

The companion history intervention uses the same structured-output schema in
history-present, history-withheld, and history-restored conditions. Its
`history_record_ids` field permits up to 16 well-formed IDs in every condition;
the owner then validates citations against the records actually delivered to
that condition. The withheld projection delivers no IDs, so a non-empty
citation is rejected as `companion_interaction_history_id_not_allowed` and
cannot count as a valid response or a history effect. The instruction already
tells the model that an empty allowlist requires an empty citation array.

Do not tighten only the withheld arm's output schema to `maxItems: 0`: that
would change the decoding constraint by condition and introduce a treatment
difference beyond history exposure. Preserve the invalid response and its
receipt-bound diagnostic as a protocol outcome. A follow-up comparison should
freeze a target record and matched task seed, verify the target appears in the
present/restored prompts, and report companion-response effects only if every
condition produces a valid owner-admitted decision. This does not establish
that the model attended to or relied on retrieved history.

`devrec-efd126272f0bbf5589590938` was not selected. The three planned held-out
probes are disjoint from the selected record's recorded examples, with zero
exact input overlap. This gives the next cycle a valid test of task performance
with a specific prior acquisition record present, but does not establish model
attention, reliance, or durable learning until the inference and scored
outcomes are observed.

This preflight also exposed a verifier bug: when no accumulated candidate
strategy exists, the report correctly uses `accumulated_strategy_not_present`
and emits no paired-ablation fields, but the verifier compared those missing
fields to prompt bindings anyway. The verifier now skips only those paired
binding comparisons for that explicit no-ablation status; actual paired
ablations retain all existing prompt and record binding checks. The plan
verifier passed, and the owner preflight verifier passed after this correction.
No inference was performed; learning, authority, and performance improvement
remain unestablished.

## Cycle after selected task-family history (2026-11-24)

Cycle `rootless-cognition-cycle-8ce186644bf74366a1f0` executed the fresh,
source-verified plan
[plan v1](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-7950cd081ae344fd89c2/plan_v1.json)
with a stable source fingerprint and governed model unload. The actual
history-present acquisition projection selected four records, including
`devrec-a2447a4bc199ed7f439a37e3`, cycle 1's own task-family record from its
3/3 acquisition. The prompt therefore carried that specific prior scored
experience into this cycle's task-family context. Model attention and reliance
remain unverified.

The fresh acquisition scored 2/3 correct in history-present, history-withheld,
and history-restored. Answerability was 2/3, 3/3, and 2/3; abstentions were
1, 0, and 1. The correctness delta was zero. Its score was selective, so no
new task-family record was admitted and the transfer stage was skipped. The
objective run's contamination assessment was `not_assessed` because leakage
from task-specific history/current context could not be verified. Thus this
run demonstrates delivery of the preceding record into the next task prompt,
but does not demonstrate improved accuracy, durable learning, or unconfounded
transfer. The [digest-bound outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-7950cd081ae344fd89c2/hermes_cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json)
binds the plan, preflight, score counts, condition record selections, and
cycle-result digest without storing model answers.

The first launch for this plan stopped before model loading because the
synthetic pre-action prompt could not fit even one complete history packet
under its 24,576-character bound. The receipt-bound diagnostic recorded a
26,774-character prompt with one compact reference-only record. I added an
explicit `withhold_developmental_history` option to the companion bridge and
resident interaction path and used it only for the synthetic pre-action
decision, whose stipulated current goal does not require developmental
history. The cycle result records that withheld posture and an empty retrieved
record list. The separate scored acquisition retained its normal history
selection, including the prior task-family record. This let the controlled
task proceed without silently dropping history from a history-present arm.

The companion response intervention still rejected its withheld response for
an unallowed history citation; it does not qualify as a response-level effect.
The synthetic fixture again reported `idle` after a `reach` prediction, so no
physical effect was established. The completed result records
`learning_established: false`, `performance_improvement_established: false`,
no authority, complete and stable source provenance, and
`governed_release_completed`.

## Acquisition probe overlap audit (2026-11-24)

The cycle's completed result showed that acquisition contamination could not be
assessed. The CPU owner preflight now reconstructs acquisition selection and
prompt binding separately from transfer selection and reports exact overlap
between planned acquisition probes and selected task-family examples. The
[post-cycle preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-1dda676be6994d52b9f2/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json)
was independently reconstructed and verified with the updated tool.

For its planned seed `455077192`, acquisition probes `[2, 6, 9]` overlap the
binder-selected `devrec-a2447a4bc199ed7f439a37e3` examples at `[2, 6]` (two
inputs). The preflight therefore marks `no_exact_history_probe_overlap` false.
Its separate held-out transfer probes `[527, 767, 890]` have zero exact overlap.
This distinction explains why transfer was preregistered as held out while the
acquisition contamination status still remained unassessed at runtime. Future
acquisition comparisons should use a preregistered task seed with zero exact
overlap in the selected history context, and retain this audit in the plan
verification path. The audit is descriptive of potential prompt leakage; it
does not establish what the model attended to.

## Acquisition-disjoint seed preparation (2026-11-24)

The owner preflight now accepts `--alternate-seed-count` (1–2048), allowing a
larger deterministic seed screen when the default 16 candidates do not produce
a history-disjoint acquisition set. The verifier reconstructs the same count
from the report and compares JSON-normalized values, preserving equivalence
between tuple-valued in-memory summaries and serialized JSON arrays. The report
is bounded by the verifier's 4 MiB custody limit; a 512-seed screen exceeded
that limit, so the reviewed screen used 32 candidates.

The verified 32-seed screen found two acquisition-disjoint seeds, `799940803`
and `799940816`. The first candidate was chosen by seed order before inference.
Its prepared cycle-0 preflight confirms acquisition probes `[5, 8, 9]` have
zero exact overlap with the selected `devrec-a2447a4bc199ed7f439a37e3` examples
`[2, 6, 7]`; the prompt is 22,172 characters, within the 24,576-character
bound. The separate transfer probes are `[279, 602, 721]`. These are prompt
contamination controls, not evidence about model attention.

The first launch stopped before inference because the WSL process could not
resolve this Windows worktree's `.git` pointer. A fresh cycle ID and plan were
created after configuring the existing source-provenance Git override through
a temporary WSL-to-Windows Git bridge. The retry had complete, stable source
provenance and reached local model inference, but stopped at the synthetic
pre-action decision: the cognition owner rejected the proposal with
`companion_interaction_decision_invalid`. The cycle is incomplete; this run
establishes neither learning nor performance improvement. The failure artifact
records `target_model_not_resident`; the final GPU sample was 0% utilization.
Preregistration timing remains unattested.

Artifacts: [32-seed screen](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-1f81a2e2670c477ca2ee/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json), [verified candidate preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-e0a9fbeef91e4c04b76c/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json), and [cycle outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-e0a9fbeef91e4c04b76c/hermes_cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json).


The failure exposed a diagnostics gap: the owner had a categorical validation
error and output digest, but the runner retained only the generic rejection
code. The runner now carries forward a bounded diagnostic containing the
validation code, digest, and optional condition/protocol identifiers; it
explicitly excludes exception text and raw model output. The earlier cycle
cannot be retroactively given this detail. Future runs need a fresh source-bound
plan because this runner change alters the experiment implementation digest.

Seed `799940816` was subsequently run and rejected at the pre-action stage. The
bounded owner diagnostic records `companion_interaction_answer_requires_evidence`;
the response attempted an answer without a citation accepted by the owner. The
[cycle summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-dc501499e03845929e14/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json)
binds that diagnostic to its failure artifact.

To give this synthetic decision an honest citable source, the runner added a
`scenario_fixture` embodiment evidence class. It records the stipulated pose,
target, objective, and transition table as a synthetic fixture input, separate
from external-world observation. The prompt asks for a citation to that fixture
and allows abstention when it is insufficient. On seed `799940824`, the owner
admitted a cited `ask` response; the runner then stopped because its pre-action
stage requires an answer before it can simulate the synthetic action. This is a
valid no-action outcome, not evidence of model failure or learning. The
[cycle summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-2b9af50fd98c4cd0a129/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json)
records the receipt digest and confirms no action was executed. The runner had
included the raw response in its exception text, although the cycle artifact
omitted it. The current code removes response text from that error path and
persists only a bounded decision diagnostic. The failure artifact now also
retains a separate pre-action outcome record for admitted `ask` and `abstain`
decisions, including their evidence IDs, response digest, and lineage status;
it explicitly records that no action was authorized or simulated. This keeps a
safe no-action result distinguishable from a rejected answer without marking
the task as completed. A later inference exercised this path; its outcome is
recorded below.

A fresh 64-seed CPU screen found seven acquisition-disjoint candidates. Seeds
`799940803`, `799940816`, and `799940824` have already had inference attempts;
seed `799940834` was first among the remaining candidates. A fresh plan was
prepared after the runner's no-action diagnostic changed its implementation
digest. Its owner preflight independently reconstructs current source, verifies
the model identity recipe and frozen history boundary, and confirms zero
historical overlap for transfer probes `[103, 616, 859]`. The 64-seed screen
for alternate seeds found 15 acquisition-disjoint candidates.

The first launch attempt stopped before cycle-root creation or model loading
because system Python lacked `psutil`. The local virtual environment was
repaired with the locked compatible runtime packages, and the same preregistered
cycle ID was then run. The owner admitted a cited `ask` with verified response
lineage. The new failure artifact records `owner_admitted_ask_no_action`, the
evidence ID and response digest, and confirms that no action was authorized or
simulated. It contains no raw response. The model was released after the run.
The cycle remains incomplete because no synthetic action or downstream
transfer trials ran; this is not a learning or task-performance result. See the
[64-seed screen](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-7fb58ec4a1ef414dbc1a/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json),
[fresh plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-9fe7e11c0d974f7180c1/plan_v1.json),

and [cycle outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-9fe7e11c0d974f7180c1/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json), which binds the external failure artifact by path and SHA-256.

A second source-bound plan used acquisition-disjoint seed `799940840` and
transfer probes `[671, 759, 836]`. Its preflight again passed source and model
identity verification with no historical overlap. The owner again admitted an
evidence-cited `ask` and stopped before action. Its response digest exactly
matches seed `799940834` even though the current evidence fact ID differs. Since
the digest binds response text, these runs produced identical ask text under
two task seeds; this weakens a seed-specific explanation but does not establish
a general model tendency. Both failure artifacts record no action, verified
lineage, no raw response, and no learning claim. Seed `799940850` later captured
the exact ask text: the model said it lacked a fresh, cited observation of the
current condition. That clarifies the issue: the scenario supplied pose, goal,
target, and transition details as a `scenario_fixture`, while the decision
request called for reasoning about a current condition. A fresh, synthetic
`sensor_observation` is a distinct evidence source and should be supplied as
such, with its synthetic provenance kept explicit.

The runner now supports one bounded clarification call after an admitted ask.
It passes the model-authored ask only into that follow-up prompt, re-presents
the current synthetic evidence, permits another ask or abstention, and records
the prompt/output/response digests plus verified receipt IDs. It can persist at
most 1,024 bytes of lineage-verified ask text as explicitly untrusted content;
it does not persist answer text or grant action authority. On seed `799940847`, the
owner again admitted an ask. The initial and clarification prompt digests differ,
but the output digests are identical; no action occurred. This shows that
re-presenting the same facts did not change the response in this run. It does
not show that the model ignored the changed prompt or establish a general
behavioral rule. The cycle remains incomplete and establishes no learning. See
the [clarification-flow plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-f6cc74a12f7b4ebfa15c/plan_v1.json),
[verified preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-f6cc74a12f7b4ebfa15c/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json),
and [outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-f6cc74a12f7b4ebfa15c/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json),
which binds the external failure artifact by path and SHA-256.
See the [second plan](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-419ff8619cb74e4abc2b/plan_v1.json),
[verified preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-419ff8619cb74e4abc2b/owner_preflight_cycle0_v1.json),
and [outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-419ff8619cb74e4abc2b/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json).

Seed `799940850` exercised bounded ask capture and the one-turn clarification.
The model's exact ask requested a fresh cited observation; it asked again after
the same fixture was re-presented. The [outcome summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-b938d0cc8d584f5b92f5/cycle0_outcome_summary_v1.json)
retains that short question as untrusted text and binds both prompts and

but the decision input exceeded the 24,576-character owner limit by 1,154
characters before inference. The [pre-inference summary](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-2d2ec39411c444f8a003/cycle0_preinference_failure_summary_v1.json)
binds that budget failure. The working runner now omits the redundant
proprioception report from the decision-time context because the scenario
