Each planned cycle measures three transfer probes. Cycles143 and144 form one
append-only history sequence, so the two slots are not independent
replications; even a repeated pattern across both can support only the
preregistered history-conditioned capability assessment, not durable learning.
The task generator now takes a transfer probe count while preserving the
three-probe default for existing plans. New Campaign71 schema-v10 plans can
freeze 4–16 probes when the cross-range outcome protocol is enabled; the CPU
preflight, sensitivity probes, owner preview, chain verifier, and runtime task
construction all consume that plan-bound count. Acquisition remains at three
probes. This change widens the scored sample without changing older plan
replays, and does not itself establish learning.

The [cycle143 owner preview](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-20261124000000000070/runtime_owner_preview_cycle143_v1.json)
passed verification with report digest
`6e2102aa918b5f613f8c1da4268fa66fee6770866a6412dba1f09eb5327a9b12`. The
new `work/verify_cross_range_history_enabled_competence_outcome.py` will, once
a scored run exists, recompute answer-free per-probe correctness and
abstention transitions from the persisted score rows, verify their score and
task digest bindings, and cross-check the condition summaries against the
cycle result, plan, and preflight. It also reads each persisted local invocation
receipt, validates its receipt digest, and binds its request and history
projection to the corresponding condition and cycle's rootless developmental
session binding. It records raw receipt file hashes
alongside the plan, preflight, cycle-result, and score-run hashes. Its verdict
is limited to internal artifact consistency; it does not attest inference
origin, establish learning, or make a causal claim. With `--output`, it also
writes a create-only audit report containing raw hashes of the plan, preflight,
cycle result, persisted score run, and condition receipts, plus a digest over
the verification report. A compatibility audit against the saved cycle46
three-condition transfer run found all three persisted receipts valid and
matched their request, output, projection, retrieved-record, and zero-overlap
summaries. That earlier result is not a Campaign70 protocol-v2 outcome. No
inference ran for Campaign70 and no Campaign70 cross-range outcome exists yet.
Cycle143 remains the first planned slot, and cycle144 needs a verified cycle143 output
boundary before continuation preflight, and model execution remains paused
while the operator uses the GPU.

### Campaign71: larger preregistered cross-range sample

Campaign71 freezes 12 wide-range transfer probes per cycle in schema-v10 plan
[plan_v1.json](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-20261124000000000071/plan_v1.json),
digest `1e48a747e0194cab60da5b5d283824e1416d2322635d9355e397c811ce5ae212`.
It binds cycles145 and146, starts from the verified cycle58 history boundary,
and retains the three-probe acquisition task. Plan verification recomputed both
model identity digests and all 2,519 implementation pins successfully. The
JSON Schema validates the frozen plan.

Cycle145's CPU owner preflight
([report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-20261124000000000071/owner_preflight_cycle145_v1.json))
was independently reconstructed with digest
`sha256:300559e949a3c6db56f36f7d1d0be9471ed435eda966fb95ae6baddd7c773d92`.
The gate is eligible, selects 12 probes
`[105, 144, 346, 439, 457, 503, 683, 758, 809, 816, 879, 994]`, and measures
zero overlap with selected historical examples across the declared task
families. The CPU runtime-owner preview
([report](../../artifacts/experiments/rootless-history-campaign-20261124000000000071/runtime_owner_preview_cycle145_v1.json))
has digest `3464eefcf85b7750d8f22382a96a9dfc72ec63f67f057b732977f6e74ce83f3d`;
it exercised the three-condition prompt and owner-to-binder path with no model
invocation or state mutation.

The first cycle145 launch stopped before model invocation and retained
`cycle-failure.json` with code
`source_provenance_incomplete_or_changed_before_inference`. The repo's
untracked experiment archive exceeded the runner's 128 MiB source fingerprint
bound, leaving source provenance incomplete. The plan slot is consumed and
must not be retried. The runner's bounded fingerprint allowance is now 256 MiB;
a read-only provenance capture completed over the current workspace with a
complete digest. Campaign72 is the fresh preregistration for another execution
attempt. Campaign71 produced no model outcome, and its two planned cycles are
not replications or evidence of learning.


### Campaign72: wider transfer projection bound

Campaign72 used the complete 256 MiB source fingerprint bound and the frozen
12-probe schema-v10 design. Its plan digest is
`0b9b703ebdb26c50693f6beb20c2b979e634624e6862f31ec2b0fb90fcfce9a2`.
Cycle147 passed model/runtime and source-provenance preflight, then completed
the planned inference work before stopping at
`objective_cross_range_history_enabled_competence` with
`run_projection_payload_over_bound`. The persisted transfer run has 12 probes
in each condition; all 12 were abstentions in history-present, withheld, and
restored conditions. The validated run artifacts therefore record no correct
transfer responses, and the cycle has no completed outcome or learning claim.

The projection failure came from duplicating withheld/restored probe rows in
the bounded developmental-history projection. The projection builder now
keeps per-probe answer-free transitions in the objective evaluation and stores
probe-result rows only for history-present when a family run has more than
three probes. CPU reconstruction of the retained cycle147 runs produced
projections of 32,367, 15,922, and 25,729 bytes, each within the 32 KiB cap.
This repairs projection capacity while retaining the comparison evidence; it
does not change the failed cycle or create an outcome that was not recorded.

### Campaign73: acquisition persisted, transfer retrieval missed

Campaign73 preregistered cycles149 and150 from the retained post-cycle147
history boundary. Cycle149 passed CPU preflight and owner preview. Its
acquisition run scored all three probes correctly in all three acquisition
conditions, and its exact source-run score projection was verified before
writeback. The resident history store then durably added acquisition record
`devrec-8d4bdcc40d2e871887858499`.

The transfer stage nevertheless omitted that newly written acquisition record.
CPU inspection of the persisted transfer run shows the record was present in
the available-history inventory; the history binder omitted it with reason
`inference_input_budget` and kept two older source-anchor records. The compact
model-facing packet for the acquisition record was 6,218 characters, compared
with 4,350 and 4,348 characters for those two anchors. The owner input cap was
24,576 characters. Persisted invocation receipts report a 23,799-character
history-present prompt with the two anchors and a 13,984-character withheld
prompt. Retaining the acquisition packet alongside both anchors would require
at least about 30,017 characters before accounting for any extra projection
metadata, so at least 5,441 characters must be recovered to fit the same
three-record history under the current cap. Most of the acquisition packet's
additional size comes from confidence-calibration and paired-confidence-history
summaries, which the current prompt-projection validators require in their full
schemas. The compaction change therefore needs a versioned, digest-linked
summary form and matching validation; deleting those fields ad hoc would break
the existing evidence checks. This locates the miss at prompt fitting, after
record retrieval, rather than at writeback or inventory admission. The run stopped
with `objective_family_transfer_history_not_retrieved`; its twelve transfer
probes were abstentions in each condition. The post-inference lineage gate
detected the omission only after the transfer inference had already been
spent. Cycle149 is consumed and cycle150 cannot be used as a continuation from
a completed cycle149 result. Neither cycle yields a transfer or learning
claim.

The runner now performs the resident owner's three-condition objective
history prompt preview before transfer inference. It checks that every
preregistered required acquisition/source record is among the selected
history-present records and stops with
`objective_family_transfer_history_not_retrievable_preinference` if not.
The preview invokes no model and records the required, selected, and missing
record IDs in the eventual cycle result when the gate passes. This is an
inference-cost and evidence-lineage guard; it does not yet make the required
history fit. The next retrieval improvement needs a smaller typed prompt
projection that retains exact score-bound input/output examples, source-score
identity, model-applicability, and confidence/uncertainty summaries while
removing redundant provenance detail already bound by the verified record
digest. Raising the character limit without measuring tokenizer and model
context headroom would not establish that the full required history can be
delivered safely. The guard has not been exercised in another model cycle.
Hermes and all other GPU-dependent runs are paused while the operator is using
the GPU. A fresh preregistration is required before any subsequent inference
attempt.

### Campaign74: compact typed confidence summaries

The numeric affine history packet now has a bounded form for score-bound
examples. It keeps the source record identity, exact input/output example,
score and model identity digests, the protocol-bound identity posture needed
to rederive applicability, candidate strategy, and task scope, while the source
record digest binds omitted custody and execution detail.
Confidence calibration is represented by a v4 prompt summary bound to a digest
of the validated v3 source report. Paired confidence history is represented by
a v3 compact summary bound to its validated v2 source report; each comparison
retains probe count, paired answered count, direction, and unsettled posture.
The projection verifier reconstructs the same typed receipt references from
these compact forms, including unsettled comparison names, so compaction does
not erase conflicting or mixed evidence from the prompt or its receipt.

CPU reconstruction of the three cycle149 history packets reduced their
serialized prompt projection from 14,916 to 11,050 characters. The acquisition
packet fell from 6,218 to 4,780 characters; the two source anchors fell from
4,350 and 4,348 to 3,136 and 3,134. Retaining source-run ID and model-identity
binding posture preserves the evidence needed to recompute strategy summaries.
This is a projection-size measurement,
not an exact governed prompt preview: dynamic confidence instructions and
accumulated strategy context also consume input budget. The 24,576-character
owner limit therefore still needs to be checked by the owner's no-inference
preview with all three records required. No model was invoked, no cycle was
started, and no transfer or learning claim follows from making the history
representation smaller. Hermes and other GPU-dependent testing remain paused
while the operator uses the GPU.

### Campaign75: budget replay with the full source-family set

The CPU budget replay now uses the transfer task's declared wide-range and
low-range history families, so the two source anchors are projected with the
same family scope as the owner. It also recomputes the compact accumulated
strategy for each candidate suffix and accounts for the confidence guidance
added by the compact calibration/effect summaries. Against the saved
23,799-character history-present prompt, the three required records project to
11,050 packet characters. Preserving source-run identity also restores the
1,051-character aggregate strategy summary; the shorter confidence guidance
uses 479 characters. The fixed-remainder estimate is 26,630 characters, 2,054
over the 24,576-character cap. Only the two-anchor suffix fits this estimate;

with digest `sha256:3c89d8725f5c2a5804ff4732f9c08fec82db2bcce9965b7145ead3c7118c0dfe`.

estimate already fails the cap. The next compaction must recover at least 2,054
characters while retaining score-bound examples, source binding, and the
aggregate strategy; the earlier pre-inference required-record gate remains in
force. No model was invoked, and this estimate alone establishes neither
transfer nor learning.

### Campaign79: bind the pre-inference transfer gate to record contents

The cycle150 owner preview previously established the exact selected IDs and
matching present/restored prompt digests, but the runner's durable gate did not
record or compare the selected record content digests against the required
source records. The transfer preflight now resolves the required records from
the writeback store and requires exact ordered ID and digest equality in both
history-present and history-restored conditions. It also validates every
record and prompt digest as a lowercase SHA-256 value before allowing the
transfer request to be staged. The gate persists required, selected, and
restored IDs and digests, both prompt digests, and the verification result.

This makes the pre-inference lineage check sensitive to content changes and
prevents an absent or malformed prompt digest from passing by equality alone.
The existing cycle150 preview predates this final source change, so that
preview's source pin did not verify the new gate. No model was invoked, and
Hermes/GPU-dependent testing remained paused. This integrity improvement
establishes no transfer outcome or developmental learning.

reconstructing the prompt. Missing required record IDs and digests are now

meaning “missing from either condition.” The diagnostic still excludes prompt
text and model outputs; inference, learning, and authority remain false on this
path. Diagnostic schema v2 includes both the budget and condition-specific
omission fields.

2,520 implementation files, the verified Hermes identity recipe, twelve

preregistration timing is not attested, and the plan grants no inference
authority.

These CPU previews establish prompt construction, record binding, and plan

### Campaign92: screen acquisition overlap and match a Qwen target

An extended CPU-only seed screen examined 64 consecutive alternate task seeds
against the frozen Cycle149 history. It found no acquisition seed with zero
overlap in the selected history prompt: four candidates overlapped two inputs
and sixty overlapped three. The acquisition inputs are sampled from the fixed
2–9 range, while the planned held-out transfer set remains disjoint from the
selected history. This does not invalidate the transfer set, but it means this
plan cannot describe its acquisition probes as wholly novel relative to
prompt-visible history. The verified 64-seed report is
`artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign75/cycle152_owner_preflight_64seed_sensitivity_v1.json`
with digest
`sha256:7b13e74d622b6621b3e2f2f4480df1627b6d2460fee144e58a6d1e9cb543863a`.
No model output was examined and no seed was selected based on behavioral
performance.

manifest is
`500a1f067a9f782620b40bee6f7b0c89e17ae61f686b92c24933e4ca4b2b8b41`, distinct
from Hermes. The [Qwen plan](../../artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign76-qwen/plan_v1.json)

13,982 withheld. The Qwen preflight binds all three source records to a model

The matched previews establish current-source history projection across a
model-artifact change, not cognitive continuity or behavioral transfer. Neither
model was loaded. Cycles 154 and 155, like 152 and 153, remain unstarted; no
inference or learning claim is made. The acquisition overlap finding is a
design constraint to resolve before describing future acquisition as novel.

### Campaign93: preserve task-family semantics while resolving acquisition overlap

The Cycle152 Hermes and Cycle154 Qwen owner preflights both confirm that the
planned acquisition probes overlap prompt-visible history. Their held-out
transfer probes remain disjoint, so the plans can support a transfer comparison
but cannot support a claim that acquisition was evaluated on wholly novel
inputs. The 64-seed sensitivity report makes the same limitation explicit for
the screened range.

Do not widen or shift the v7 acquisition interval in place. The family ID,
probe ranges, history-family declarations, campaign plan versions, runtime
gates, and offline overlap reconstruction jointly define v7. Reusing that ID
with new ranges would make old and new plans look semantically identical while
their task-generation contracts differ. The existing campaigns remain valid as
registered; their acquisition overlap must stay visible in interpretation.

A future novelty-focused task family needs a new ID and a coordinated protocol
revision. Its preregistration must bind both acquisition and transfer ranges,
keep the affine mapping fixed, require acquisition probes to be disjoint from
the selected history projection, and retain the existing exact transfer
non-overlap audit. CPU preflight should reject an acquisition set that fails
that condition before any model is loaded. The planned v7 transfer work can
continue to answer its narrower held-out transfer question, but it must not be
reported as novel-acquisition evidence. No GPU inference was performed.

### Campaign94: preregister v8 novel acquisition and transfer

A new task-family version addresses Campaign92's acquisition overlap without
changing v7. The registered mapping remains `output = 2 * input + 1`; v8 samples
three acquisition inputs from 1001–10000 and three transfer inputs from
10001–1000000. The campaign plan uses schema v11 and pins the model identity
and 2520-file experiment implementation. Its CPU planner also rejects exact
probe reuse between planned cycles.

The runner and offline owner preflight now require the v8 acquisition probes
to have complete, zero-overlap measurements against selected history from the
v6, v7, and v8 families. The runtime check runs before inference; the campaign
chain verifier independently recomputes acquisition overlap from the prior
history boundary. A failed acquisition novelty check prevents the planned
comparison from proceeding.

### Campaign96: persist the acquisition novelty decision in cycle evidence

The v8 runtime preflight now emits receipt schema v3. Alongside transfer
history overlap, it binds the acquisition overlap measurement and the boolean
`novel_acquisition_qualified` into the preflight digest. The receipt is formed
before the novelty gate raises, so a rejected acquisition set can leave an
explicit incomplete-cycle artifact with its measured overlap and a false
qualification value. The [v3 schema](rootless_novel_acquisition_transfer_probe_preflight.schema.json)
requires zero overlap for qualification and preserves a false result when the
measurement is incomplete or finds reuse. The campaign chain verifier checks
these fields; when the prior history bytes are available from a completed cycle
or seed bundle, it independently recomputes the overlap.

An offline reconstruction script now rebuilds the planned Cycle168 v3

cross-environment verification, transfer the original digest-matched manifests
into the Windows experiment custody root and provide the verifier dependency.
No model was loaded, no inference was run, and the campaign remains unstarted.

### Campaign105: compare the history delivered to both target prompts

The [runtime-preview parity audit](../../artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign87-v8-hermes-12probe/matched_runtime_owner_preview_parity_v1.json)
joins the verified Cycle176 Hermes and Cycle178 Qwen no-inference previews to
the matched pair. Both targets' present and restored conditions deliver the
same two record IDs and digests, with matching score-bound example digests,
source-run digests, and score digests. The withheld condition is empty in both
previews. The shared 12 transfer probes and condition order are bound to the
pair.

The owner preflight had selected four records; the prompt binder projected two
of them to each target. The parity audit makes that distinction explicit.
Although the score-bound source records and examples match, their projected
prompt-packet digests and task digests differ across targets. Source inspection
explains the expected differences: the history packet adds applicability
metadata for the target model identity, and each seeded task commits its public
task digest to a fresh private nonce. The registered task family, generation
seeds, backend seeds, and requested probes match. Full prompt digests differ
and prompt sizes match. The previews do not export full prompt text, so this
audit establishes parity of delivered scored-history evidence and task
inputs, not identical prompt serialization or model attention. A future
cross-model replication can remove the nonce-derived text variation by sharing
one secret task-commitment nonce across its targets while retaining that nonce
in scoring custody. The task-pair factory now accepts an optional private
acquisition/transfer nonce pair for independently constructed matched plans;
omitting it preserves the existing fresh-nonce behavior. Campaign87/88 remain
unchanged and retain their target-specific commitments, so this is a path for
a newly frozen pair rather than a retrospective correction. The audit also
reconstructs both nonce-free task
specifications from each shared generation seed using the plan-pinned task
factory. It confirms the task specifications and seed-zero probe list match
without publishing the oracle parameters or commitment nonce. A pinned-source
runtime replay receipt verifies both saved preview payloads against the source
closure and plan inputs, and confirms the preview implementation matches the
pinned implementation. The receipt does not attest where the original preview
reports came from. No model was loaded, no inference occurred, and learning or
transfer remains unestablished.

The historical-examples preflight can now consume the same private key file
for each target using `--shared-task-commitment-key-file`. The key tool
[`create_task_commitment_key.py`](../../scripts/create_task_commitment_key.py)
creates a 0600, create-only 256-bit key without printing its contents. The
preflight derives role-separated nonces from the key, task family, and task
seed; its report includes only the key-file hash and a digest of the derived
nonce pair. The preflight verifier must receive the same key file to rebuild
the saved report. New v7 preflight reports bind the task factory, preflight,
and resident cognition implementation hashes. When a plan's only stale
identity-source pin is the campaign planner, `--allow-recomputed-identity-recipe`
permits an explicitly recorded replay only if current code rederives every
planned identity digest exactly; all other source-pin mismatches still reject.
Both original and replay source hashes are retained in the report.

After both target preflights are frozen, the CPU-only
[matched commitment verifier](../../analysis/verify_matched_task_commitment_pair.py)
reopens the same private key, checks that both reports bind it, the same
seed-scoped nonce pair, the same cycle root and source receipt, and identical
current task-factory, preflight, and resident cognition source hashes. It also
checks each target's plan-bound identity replay evidence and recomputes the
registered task's public digest. Its self-digested receipt contains the shared
source binding, report/source/key hashes, task digest, and target identity
digests; it does not contain the key, nonce values, oracle parameters, or model
outputs. Use
`--left-report`, `--right-report`, `--key-file`, and an optional create-only
`--output` path. The [closed output schema](matched_task_commitment_pair_verification.schema.json)
defines the receipt shape and no-inference posture. Verification now requires
each preflight to explicitly bind `model_loaded`, `model_invoked`,
`inference_performed`, `runtime_condition_created`, and
`learning_established` to false, alongside `authority`. Missing or positive
flags reject the pair, so the receipt cannot infer a no-inference posture from
only the final learning claim. It also requires both previews to bind the same
cycle root and source receipt digest. Campaign87/88 predate this protocol and
are not expected to pass it.

For a new pair, create one pair-scoped key in private WSL custody and pass
that same path to both target preflight commands and each later
`--verify-report` command:

```bash
python3 scripts/create_task_commitment_key.py \
  --output "$HOME/.local/state/matched-v7-task-commitment.key"
```

Do not copy the key into the repository or publish it with the preflight
reports. The task-plan journal stores the derived transfer nonce in scoring
custody so the frozen task can be reconstructed after restart.

To repeat the read-only parity check without writing another receipt:

```powershell
py analysis/verify_matched_runtime_owner_previews.py --pair artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign87-v8-hermes-12probe/matched_model_target_pair_v2.json --left-preview artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign87-v8-hermes-12probe/cycle176_runtime_owner_preview_v1.json --right-preview artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign88-v8-qwen-12probe/cycle178_runtime_owner_preview_v1.json --pinned-preview-replay artifacts/experiments/resident-history-budget-campaign87-v8-hermes-12probe/matched_model_target_runtime_preview_replay_v1.json
```

### Campaign106: freeze a cross-model shared-task preflight pair

The CPU-only [matched task commitment pair](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v4/matched_pair_verification_v2.json)
verifies two v7 historical-examples preflights against the same completed
source cycle, `rootless-cognition-cycle-20261124000000000009`, and the same
source receipt digest. One target identity was reconstructed from the saved
Hermes 4 plan and the other from the saved Qwen 3 plan. Both model identity
recipes were replayed with current code and reproduced their plan's identity
digests exactly; the plans' only stale identity implementation pin was the
campaign planner source. The reports preserve both the old pin and the replay
source hashes. No model was loaded for this reconstruction.

Both preflights use task family
`rootless_integer_affine_transfer_wide_range_v7` and seed `13688478`. They
share task digest
`sha256:baa38da07a3d7d31aea4f8dfe38ff6b0c0c2da08284050210212d7cecd4f77dc`
and nonce-pair digest
`sha256:07875fdaf93d75927330005ade4094e3407aff2beea3c3f9e116c975ffcedb20`,
while binding distinct model identity digests. Each saved preflight was
reconstructed from the private task journal and source history. The pair
receipt confirms the same source, matching task commitment, no inference, no
learning claim, and no authority. The private key remains outside the
repository; reports contain only its digest.

The digest-only [prompt parity audit](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v4/matched_prompt_parity_v2.json)
is produced by the CPU-only
[`audit_matched_task_commitment_prompt_parity.py`](../../analysis/audit_matched_task_commitment_prompt_parity.py)
and conforms to the [v2 audit schema](matched_task_preflight_prompt_parity_v2.schema.json).
It shows that Hermes selected three scored history records and Qwen selected two.
The two shared records have identical source-record digests; one additional
record was selected only for Hermes. Both arms withheld the same six examples
by digest, while both included-arm and omitted-arm prompt digests differ across
targets. The audit makes no claim about prompt-text equality or model
attention. The Hermes-only record is a wide-range example whose recorded source
model identity digest matches the Hermes target and not the Qwen target. This
is consistent with model identity binding affecting retrieval, but does not
prove the selection rule or model use. Because selected record sets differ,
these previews are not a matched-history performance comparison; a future
comparison needs a fixed shared history set or a separately preregistered
selection policy.

The new v7 report schema also requires explicit `inference_performed: false`
and `runtime_condition_created: false`, alongside the model-load, model-call,
learning, and authority flags. During preflight replay, an owner validation
bug was corrected: negative-example guidance must match the final projection
for included and negative-guidance arms, while the raw-examples omission arm
must not retain guidance metadata from the pre-ablation prompt. These checks
validate preflight comparability only. No target model was invoked, so this
pair provides no evidence of learning, transfer, or surviving cognitive
continuity.

### Campaign107: constrain both previews to the same verified history

The v4 parity audit showed that target-conditioned retrieval selected different
history sets, so the next CPU-only step froze their intersection as an explicit
record allowlist. The [allowlist](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v5/shared_history_allowlist.json)
binds the v4 source cycle and receipt, task digest, and each included record ID
and source digest. Its semantic digest is
`sha256:c37ad5b231c97398740a31073b2527138b32bd765c9fcf44bbc43cd4969e0829`.
Both v5 preflights consumed that same file and the same v4 task commitment.
Their independently replayed owner previews selected exactly the same two
records with matching source digests.

The [v5 pair receipt](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v5/matched_pair_verification_v3.json)
now binds both the semantic allowlist digest and the exact allowlist file hash.
The [v5 parity audit](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v5/matched_prompt_parity_v3.json)
also checks those bindings against each preflight before comparing selection.
This closes a provenance gap: pair equality is tied to the actual frozen list
used by both previews, not just to a matching list label. The optional output
fields are defined in the pair-verification and parity v2 schemas.

This controls selected history content for a future cross-model comparison;
it does not make target-specific prompts byte-identical, prove either model
attended to the records, or establish learning or transfer. Both v5 reports
remain CPU preflights with no model load, inference, runtime condition, learning
claim, or authority. The parity audit reports unequal prompt digests for both
the included and omitted arms, despite identical task commitments and selected
history. Source inspection identifies a target-specific active-model identity
digest in each model-visible history projection as a remaining prompt confound.
The new [parity readiness audit](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261124-v5/matched_prompt_parity_v4.json)
replays the sealed inputs and records
`cross_model_prompt_comparison_ready: false` with posture
`blocked_model_visible_prompt_digest_mismatch`; its digest is
`sha256:e81756dcd1be77b654a4102a751578d1040d10f021e2799c075c746c73b20f45`.
The auditor now makes this gate explicit in
[`audit_matched_task_commitment_prompt_parity.py`](../../analysis/audit_matched_task_commitment_prompt_parity.py),
with its [v3 report schema](matched_task_preflight_prompt_parity_v3.schema.json).

The v4 audit schema adds a source-to-target applicability parity precondition.
For each selected record it records whether each bound source identity matches
the left and right target, then requires those relations to agree before prompt
digest parity can mark a pair ready. It fails closed when a selected source
identity is missing or the two target relations differ. The existing v5 report
remains the earlier v3 audit result and remains blocked on prompt digest
mismatch; it has not been rewritten or promoted by this schema change.

The opt-in policy is now bound by the objective plan digest. The resident
history binder removes target identity digests from model-visible history and
removes the target identity and target-conditioned retrieval-policy ID from
the top-level prompt context. It fails closed unless every selected scored
example has a verified source identity distinct from that target. Under that
condition the model-visible `different_active_model_identity` category and
its score-bound summaries are common across a matched pair. The controller
request evidence retains the full target identity, source identity digests,
and each recomputed relation in the [identity binding v1 schema](history_prompt_identity_binding_v1.schema.json);
the binder also checks that the target digest does not occur in the final
prompt. It adds a shared conservative instruction
that this applicability does not establish target-model performance, learning,
or transfer.

The parity audit v4 schema adds the source-applicability equality condition
described above. Before using a pair for cross-model inference, rebuild both
CPU preflights under the policy and require equal prompt digests within each
arm plus the existing shared-history and shared-task checks. This is a new
protocol: Campaign87/88 and matched-task-commitment v5 remain unchanged and do
not satisfy it. No model was loaded and no inference occurred. GPU inference
stays paused while the GPU is in use.

When this v5 record was first written, the objective-task descriptor accepted
`matched_cross_model_identity_omitted:v1` only for family contract v6 with
required task-family history, but the policy had not yet been exercised in a
CPU preflight. That gap is closed by the later identity-neutral preflight
below. The original v5 pair remains ineligible because its reports predate the
policy and its prompt digests differ.

### 2026-11-25: identity-neutral matched CPU preflight

The policy is now exercised against the same v7 wide-range objective task for
the archived Hermes and Qwen targets. Initial no-policy previews showed that
the targets selected different histories; a verified intersection allowlist
therefore freezes the two shared records before the policy-enabled previews.
The runtime recomputes each source-to-current-target relation from the
controller-validated source and target identity digests. This avoids trusting
an experience relation that was formed against a different historical target.
Missing, malformed, or same-target source identity still fails closed.

Both v8 CPU reports were independently reconstructed from their journals. The
[shared-history allowlist](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261125-identity-neutral-v5/shared_history_allowlist.json),
[v3 pair receipt](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261125-identity-neutral-v5/matched_pair_verification_v3.json),
and [v4 parity audit](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261125-identity-neutral-v5/matched_prompt_parity_v4.json)
verify the common two-record history set, matching withheld examples, distinct
target identities, equal controller binding per arm, cross-model source
applicability, and equal model-visible prompt digests for both arms. Audit
digest: `sha256:33bbf4655a8858a65f42da10f1d91b21e1d4cb6da35b0ca15d2b8c68dfecde85`.
The target identity remains in controller evidence and is omitted from the
model-visible prompt. Model load, invocation, inference, learning, and authority
are all false. Prompt digest equality establishes input parity only; it does
not establish model attention, improved performance, transfer, or learning.

The governed pair runner now requires the parity audit and shared allowlist
paths for identity-neutral tasks. Before model load, it rechecks both canonical
digests, the allowlist file digest cited by the audit, task and target binding,
and exact ordered record IDs and digests against the frozen execution protocol.
The resulting proof is included in run resource evidence. This prevents a later
scored run from silently using a different history set than the CPU parity
reproduction; it does not establish behavioral parity or learning.

The preflight run also found and fixed policy restoration across objective
request reconstruction and private journal reload. The private task-plan record
now has a v2 form that durably stores the policy under the [closed task-plan
schema](historical_examples_private_task_plan_v2.schema.json); v1 plans still
load as policy-free tasks. The final replay reconstructs the policy from the
private journal without a preflight-side reattachment. The identity-recipe replay gate
now permits only the reviewed source-path set when recomputing archived target
identities, and still requires the recomputed identity outputs to match the
plan. Hermes/GPU testing remains paused while the GPU is in use.

Task-plan storage is keyed by both the oracle digest and private plan digest,
so a policy-enabled plan and a policy-free control can share the same oracle in
one journal without colliding. The [CPU coexistence
preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261125-identity-neutral-v6-coexistence/hermes_control_preflight.json)
and [policy replay](../../artifacts/experiments/matched-task-commitment-20261125-identity-neutral-v6-coexistence/hermes_policy_verification.json)
reconstructed both plans with the same task digest and distinct private plan
digests. Legacy v1 path lookup remains available for existing journals.

The follow-up v4 audit now binds every controller ledger row to an exact source
fact, task digest, source identity, and selected record digest reconstructed
from the cycle store. Runtime projection also fails closed if any selected
record lacks a non-empty, fully bound task-family experience. The private
scoring key and answer-bearing journals for this reproduction are retained in
restricted WSL state rather than the repository, so later scoring can reuse the
same committed task. These stronger bindings still establish only comparable
inputs; model behavior and durable learning require a later governed run.

### 2026-11-25: rootless strategy-pair execution handoff

The rootless Ollama cognition runner now accepts a frozen strategy-inclusion
preflight through `SENTIENTOS_HERMES_STRATEGY_INCLUSION_PREVIEW_PATH` and its
private journal through `SENTIENTOS_HERMES_STRATEGY_INCLUSION_JOURNAL_ROOT`.
The configured prior-history root must be the exact history directory named by
the preflight source cycle, and the preregistration digest must equal the frozen
protocol digest. Before allocating a run, the runner reloads and verifies the
protocol, private task, answer-key commitment, source receipt, selected history,
and target-model identity. After governed model setup it reconstructs the
owner prompt pair from the copied history and checks its frozen task, target,
history, strategy, generation, and prompt bindings against the preflight. The
request digest comparison removes only the per-run rootless session-custody
binding; every other request field remains included, and the receipt's actual
session binding is independently verified. Both arms then run sequentially
through the persisted strategy-pair coordinator, which verifies invocation
receipts before scoring and stores the score in the private journal.

This closes the runner dispatch gap for the accumulated-strategy intervention.
The runner was syntax-parsed only in this GPU-in-use window; no arm was executed,
no model was loaded, and no learning or improvement claim is made. A later
available-GPU run is still required to test the behavioral comparison.
The runner also recovers a claimed arm after interruption by searching bounded
prior rootless-cycle receipt stores for its session-normalized frozen request
identity, then reopening each candidate through historical rootless receipt
verification and the strategy-arm receipt validator. The scan is limited to
512 cycle roots and 16,384 receipt files. Ambiguous, missing, or over-limit
matches fail closed, so restart recovery cannot silently issue a duplicate arm
call.

### 2026-11-25: versioned strategy request-digest postures

The rootless strategy runner needs a preregistered request digest that remains
stable across session restarts without weakening authority checks. Strategy
protocol v5 bound `request_semantics_with_rootless_session_custody_elided:v1`,
which removes only per-run session custody. Runtime evidence then showed that
the request also contains the full authority-map digest, whose map payload
includes a changing observation timestamp. Protocol v6 therefore binds
`request_semantics_with_rootless_session_custody_and_volatile_authority_map_digest_elided:v2`.
Its canonical digest removes the session-custody field and the volatile full
map digest, while retaining the stable `authority_map_policy_digest`. The
persisted governed receipt still binds and verifies the actual full authority
map digest, policy digest, and active model identity. Protocols v2 through v5
retain their previous exact-digest semantics.

available-GPU run.

The [matched-task analysis protocol](strategy_inclusion_matched_task_analysis_protocol_v1.json)
is frozen before inference and digest-bound from the three-instance series
record. Its unit of analysis is the task instance: report each included-minus-
omitted exact-count difference separately, then aggregate all nine probes only
if all three pairs have valid receipt-bound scores. Abstentions stay in the
fixed denominator and are reported separately; a missing or invalid arm makes
that pair incomplete rather than a zero or an exclusion. Report descriptive
counts only, with no p-value or significance claim. Even a consistent
contrast supports only task-bounded strategy-conditioned behavior; a later
held-out session without the strategy prompt is still required to assess
durability.
The CPU-only [series analyzer](../../work/analyze_strategy_inclusion_preflight_series.py)
consumes a runtime-input manifest with one entry per frozen task seed and
cycle, plus its cycle root, journal root, attempt status, and any failure
code. It reopens the per-task preflight and verification artifacts, checks the
runner binding, runtime protocol, stored score digest, and both persisted arm
receipt bindings, then reports each task's correct/incorrect/abstained counts
and paired transitions. It reads answer-free score outcomes only. Incomplete
or invalid pairs remain in the report and suppress the primary aggregate.
Syntax compilation and CLI help passed; no completed behavioral scores exist
yet, so end-to-end analysis remains pending those runs.

### Evidence gate for the prepared three-probe strategy-inclusion pair

The v11 protocol is intentionally a first behavioral manipulation check. Its
three transfer probes estimate behavior on one frozen task instance; they do
not provide independent replications, a stable estimate across task draws, or
evidence of developmental change. A successful included-arm result by itself
must therefore be reported only as task-bound performance. An included-versus-
omitted difference is interpretable as an intervention contrast only if both
arms complete with valid receipts under the same frozen protocol and scoring
rules; an incomplete or invalid arm yields no contrast.

Before describing the result as transferable strategy use, repeat the paired
contrast on independently generated task instances and seeds, retain the same
single-field intervention, and report every preregistered pair including
failures and abstentions. Before describing it as durable learning, additionally
show that the effect persists in a later session without re-presenting the
strategy and transfers to held-out task instances. These are future evidence
requirements, not results of v11. No model call is needed to establish this
interpretation boundary.

### Can a fresh ordinary chat see the prior number-task experience?

On November 25, a CPU replay prepared the same ordinary-chat question with two
fresh transcripts. One resident reopened a temporary copy of the 30-record
archive used for the v11 pilot. The other started with an empty store. Both
used the same receipt-bound target identity, empty canonical-memory input and
empty runtime context. Each used the resident's normal question ranking with
no record allowlist and no configured semantic encoder. This check ran the
history provider, the chat budget calculation and the prompt assembler.
It stopped before inference.

The question asks for outputs at 367, 619 and 883. It gives no rule, worked
examples, historical answers or record IDs. None of those inputs occurred in
the eligible scored rows projected from the archive's two registered task
families or in the earlier three-probe pilot. Sixteen archived records
contained projected rows. Of the eight retrieved records, the first
task-bearing record occurred fourth in the list the earlier chat binder
consumed. These are three inputs in one question.

| Measurement on the archived store | Before repair | After repair |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Space available for developmental history, characters | 2,286 | 3,100 |
| Minimum space the owner needed to return one ranked record | 7,722 | 5,072 |
| Guidance supplied with that record, characters | 3,016 | 366 |
| Records reaching the ordinary prompt | 0 | 0 |

The old guidance exceeded the chat reader's 2,000-character limit on its own.
The resident now adds embodiment guidance when a selected packet carries an
embodiment report. Ordinary chat also spends fewer characters on instructions
when canonical memory is empty. A focused calibration regression covers a
single admitted record reaching an ordinary prompt under 8,000 characters.
The historical replay verifier also accepts the resident writer's tagged
omission digest and verifies its contents. Earlier untagged chat digests
continue to verify.

The post-repair size diagnostic found 3,061 characters of selection metadata
beside a 1,422-character array for the first record the binder tried. The full
prompt with that one record would take 9,976 characters. Under the actual
ceiling, the archived and empty conditions produced byte-identical prompts.
A Hermes comparison on this pair cannot measure the behavioral effect of the
archived experience. The next experiment needs to show that task-relevant
evidence survives both ranking and prompt assembly before scoring responses.
The empty-store comparison checks delivery only. A developmental comparison
would also need matched prior histories that differ in the experience under study.

Run the current version from the worktree in WSL with
`python -m work.preflight_ordinary_chat_retention <archived-cycle-root>`,
using the root of cycle `rootless-cognition-cycle-20261124000000000009`.
The reports retain the input sets, ranked record IDs, inventory and prompt
digests, and the separate enlarged-budget diagnostic. [Before repair](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/before.json) · [After repair](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/after.json)

### Scored observations in a fresh chat

The next CPU replay used the same 30-record archive, target identity and original
question. Ordinary chat now indexes the type of a previously scored integer
prediction and its task family when the question asks about that kind of task.
The ranking uses the presence of a verified score, independent of its outcome.
The selected record supplies the historical input, observed answer, evaluator
verdict and abstention flag for each probe in the run. The packet identifies
the family, run, source fact, score digest and source condition. The question
supplies the new inputs. Neither supplies a fitted rule or a new target answer.

The v4 selection format records omitted content hashes once. Reconstructing the
ordered references from the hashes and the selected records preserves the
omission commitment. Earlier stored selections retain their original format.
The compact format and shorter provenance instructions leave space for the
observations under the ordinary chat limit. The shared ranker puts the strongest
match last for consumers that keep the end of the list. The v2 chat selection
policy reverses that order before taking the prefix that fits. The older
reports list candidates in the order the binder attempted them. The separate
companion intervention now carries the same retrieval policy from preflight
into all three conditions.

This replay called `PersistentConversationService.chat` with a new session and
empty canonical memory for each request. The real resident bridge supplied
history through the ordinary fallback route. The service assembled its own
runtime context and request linkage. A capture adapter stopped at the point
where the service would call inference. The source score was checked against
the rows parsed from the finished prompt.

| Fresh-chat condition | Prompt characters | Scored examples reaching the inference boundary |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Original wording, either probe set | 7,410 | 3 → 4, 4 → 5, 8 → 9. All three marked incorrect |
| Empty history, same question | 3,949 | None |
| Reopened archive, original question and inputs | 7,410 | Byte-identical to the first archived prompt |
| Reworded arithmetic exercise, either probe set | 7,383 | 2 → 5, 6 → 13, 7 → 15. All three marked correct |
| Question about predicting dropped frames | 3,866 | None |

We crossed two wordings with two probe sets (367, 619, 883 and 101, 257, 811).
Changing only the wording changed the record delivered for both sets. Changing
only the inputs preserved the record for both wordings. For each of the four
questions, removing the history block reproduced its empty-store prompt
exactly. All six target inputs were disjoint
from the two families' projected historical rows and the earlier pilot inputs.
The incorrect rows came from the same recorded active model identity as the
target. The correct rows came from a different recorded identity. Identity
includes configuration, so a difference alone cannot establish a change of
weights. Both wording and available evidence matter to any eventual score.
In particular, the original wording exposed only rejected historical answers.

A separate control requested one specific record containing adverse outcomes.
Projection preserved the recorded incorrect answer at input 23 and the
abstentions at 394 and 499. The ordinary retrieval trials used automatic
selection. This rehearsal establishes evidence delivery and exact reopening
for these prompts. Behavioral retention, attribution to the acquisition
experience and transfer across model replacements still require measured
responses under matched histories. [Service replay and source bindings](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/scored_rows_service_v2.json)

### Ordinary chat after two different acquisitions from one starting history

Two archived residents inherited the same 39 records and writeback receipts,

scored wide-range arithmetic exercise. The left run stopped at a later
writeback when its history projection exceeded the size limit. Its acquisition
had already committed. The replay reopened each addition, verified its
invocation receipts and checked the individual rows against its scored run.
The two acquisition runs used different seeds and model configurations.

Before the retrieval change, asking about the most recent scored wide-range
exercise surfaced the same older inputs (23, 394, 499) in all three stores.
Ordinary chat now uses verified checkpoint order when a question explicitly
asks for the latest scored arithmetic exercise. A later citation of an
already admitted run preserves its original place in that order. An explicit
range limits the eligible exercises. [Before the change](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/sibling_history_before.json)

| Resident | Scored answer delivered for the wide-range question | Recorded abstentions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared parent | Answered 5 for input 2, scored correct | 7 and 8 |
| Left child | Answered 26 for input 6, scored incorrect | 7 and 8 |
| Right child | Answered 26 for input 5, scored incorrect | 7 and 9 |

The low-range question selected the same inherited record in all three stores.

### CPU behavioral replay of the sibling histories

Qwen2.5 1.5B Instruct ran through the ordinary chat service and its local
inference governor. Both acquisition branches, the common parent and the
empty control used the same evaluation model, seed, two CPU threads and
fresh conversation sessions. The evaluation model differed from the archived
acquisition model. The runner recorded each rendered prompt and expected
score by digest before inference, checked the admitted receipt against the
prompt, and observed zero resident model bytes in VRAM after each reply.
The private scoring profiles came from the original acquisition runs.

In the first seven calls, every delivered reply was an empty JSON array in
a Markdown fence. This included the two different wide-range records, the
parent, both shared low-range controls, the empty store and a repeat of the
left wide-range question. The specified bare-array format scored zero exact
answers. Even ignoring the fence, all six history-bearing calls omitted the
recorded observations. This experiment had a conflict between its requested
array format and the resident's instruction to cite the source record.
[Frozen array protocol](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-array/plan.json)
and [responses with governed receipts](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-array/result.json).

During setup, an inherited low-range score was reopened from the originating
cycle, and prompt capture was aligned with the live adapter's model and
workload metadata. Neither mismatch reached inference. A separate initial
CPU invocation timed out at 30 seconds while processing the prompt. The
completed experimental run used a 120-second service budget.

A second run preserved the cited format and supplied the inline control as a
short plain-text report. The model returned eight replies. Independent replay
verified each ordinary chat receipt, prompt digest, source score and model
configuration. It cited the record shown in each sibling prompt. However,
its rows used placeholder inputs such as `input_integer_1` and sometimes
marked a recorded abstention incorrect. The shared low-range result also
differed from the automatic retrieval capture. On the direct report control,
the outcomes were correct but the input values were strings. Asked about an
absent history, the model stated it could not find the exercise in session
history. These outcomes show sensitivity to history availability and format,
alongside unreliable transcription of observed evidence. [Cited protocol](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-cited-v2/plan.json)
and [verified replies](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-cited-v2/result.json).

The cited protocol's scoring criterion allows a single fenced JSON object, but
the original scorer passed the fence directly to `json.loads`, so it marked
every fenced answer invalid before checking its contents. The offline
[protocol-correct regrade](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/sibling-chat-packet-regrade-v1/report.json)
first reruns the original receipt, prompt, source-score, and delivery verifier,
then removes only the one fence allowed by the frozen protocol. This does not
change the original trial artifact. The corrected regrade still finds zero
exact model answers across eight trials: the replies contain placeholder or
missing input values, incorrect row types or outcomes; some also fail the
record citation requirement. A deterministic copy of each already verified
history projection scores exactly on all seven applicable history and empty
history trials. The eighth trial is an inline report control, so it is excluded
from the history packet copy. This control establishes that the exposed packet
contains enough structured information for exact transcription; it is not a
model response and provides no evidence of learning. No inference was run to
produce this diagnostic. The [regrade implementation](../../work/regrade_sibling_chat_packet.py)
records both measures separately and retains hashes of the original plan and
results. The reusable scorer now implements the declared fenced-object rule
for new cited runs under protocol v3. The verifier keeps protocol v2's original
scoring when checking its saved result, so historical scores remain
reproducible and are not silently rewritten. No new model call is needed to
apply this correction.

The extraction intervention was first tried in cited protocol v4 by appending
the mapping to the history context. That rendered the mapping twice: once in
history guidance and again in serialized context. Its first generation exceeded
the matched 120-second CPU timeout before a trial result was saved, so it
provides no evidence of improved extraction.

Cited protocol v5 injects the same mapping once at the model prompt boundary,
after retrieval and context validation. It leaves the selected history,
selection digest, and retrieval question unchanged. The new
`--preflight-only` mode saves the fully captured plan and an explicit no-inference
status record without starting generation. The independently verified
[v5 preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-explicit-mapping-preflight-v5/)
contains eight planned trials: five history exposures each retain the same
single scored record as the archived comparison, and the empty-history controls
remain empty. Prompts range from 7,812 to 7,819 characters. The pinned model was
not resident and inference was not performed.
The first full v5 attempt used the unchanged two-thread, 120-second CPU
configuration and timed out on its first trial after 120,361 ms. Its governed
receipt records a terminated timeout, zero output bytes, no fallback, and zero
VRAM according to the isolated server's live placement report. The
[timeout artifact](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-explicit-mapping-timeout-v5/)
passes an independent receipt verifier. The runner now preserves failure
records automatically; the record for this first attempt was reconstructed
from its governed receipt. At this point the matched model comparison remained
outstanding; the timeout is not evidence for or against improved extraction or
learning. The GPU follow-up is recorded below.

To distinguish an oversized-model problem from a broader CPU-runtime constraint,
the runner now accepts a second locally pinned model, `qwen3:0.6b`. Separate
control and mapped preflights use the same model and configuration digest,
identical matched base prompts, and the same selected evidence. Their maximum
prompt sizes are 7,648 and 7,801 characters. The full no-mapping control also
timed out at 120,339 ms with zero output bytes. Because the control produced no
answer, the mapping arm was not run. These records show that a smaller model
does not resolve the current CPU timeout; they establish no result about
cross-model cognitive continuity or the mapping intervention. Both preflights
and the timeout receipt are in the
[qwen3 probe artifact](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cpu-sibling-recall-qwen3-06b-probe-v1/).

## Matched Hermes CUDA recall probe (2026-11-26)

The ordinary-chat retrieval comparison resumed on an isolated WSL Ollama
runtime after the Windows runner was confirmed to remain CPU-only. The pinned
`sentientos-hermes4:14b-q4km` artifact is Qwen3 14.8B Q4_K_M, manifest
`f6a766d405cbb73f9ba64a59b8fb29a3418d0da0b6fad15a0ca70687413ce0ce`. Ollama
reported 24 of 41 layers offloaded to the RTX 3060; each completed trial recorded
5,416,643,072 bytes of model residency in VRAM. The model server used a private
one-model store. This confirms CUDA execution for these probes, not GPU-backed
SentientOS ownership.

An empty-history inline diagnostic returned syntactically valid JSON in
59,400 ms. It reproduced the three inline rows but returned `record_id: null`
instead of `inline-example`; the independent exact score was false. This showed
that output shape and exact evidence attribution are separate requirements.
That diagnostic's governed inference completed, but an outdated CPU-only
residency assertion stopped the runner before it finalized the result. The
result was reconstructed from the persisted assistant transcript and governed
receipt, then passed the independent diagnostic verifier. The matched arms ran
with the corrected placement check.

The independent control and explicit-mapping preflights each captured eight
trials and five single-record history exposures. They used the same model
identity, runtime, generation settings, selected history evidence, and matched
base prompts. Maximum prompt lengths were 7,665 and 7,818 characters. The full
arms completed all eight governed calls and passed the independent receipt and
answer regrader:

- The no-mapping control scored 3/8 exact: `left_low`, `empty_wide`, and
  `parent_wide`.
- Explicit field-to-row mapping scored 4/8 exact: `right_wide`, `left_low`,
  `empty_wide`, and `right_low`.
- Mapping changed two failures to exact answers (`right_wide`, `right_low`),
  while `parent_wide` changed from exact to malformed. Both repeated `left_wide`
  trials and the inline condition failed the official full-output format or
  citation criteria in at least one arm.

This is a small, mixed result: one more exact answer in the mapped arm, with a
regression in another history condition. It supports the narrower claim that an
explicit mapping instruction can change how this model uses retrieved history
in ordinary chat. It does not establish durable learning, transferable
improvement, model-independent continuity, or a general performance gain. The
next useful step is an independent replication on a fresh task family with a
held-out transfer condition.

The [control preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-control-preflight-v3/),
[mapping preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-mapping-preflight-v3/),
[empty-inline diagnostic](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-empty-inline-diagnostic-v1/),
[control run](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-control-full-v1/),
and [mapping run](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-mapping-full-v1/)
contain independently verifiable plans and results plus checksummed complete
capture archives.

### Post-hoc diagnosis of cited-output failures

The saved full outputs were independently re-opened with
[`analyze_sibling_chat_recall_outputs.py`](../../work/analyze_sibling_chat_recall_outputs.py).
This diagnostic first reruns the original verifiers and confirms the matched
model, configuration, prompts, and source evidence. It then separately parses
the single fenced JSON object, records whether prose occurs outside it, and
compares the fenced rows and citation with each trial's expected answer. It
does not change or replace the official full-response score.

The original score remains control 3/8 and mapping 4/8. In a post-hoc
fenced-content regrade, control is 4/8 and mapping is 6/8 exact. Five control
outputs and four mapping outputs have prose outside their JSON fence, which
explains why some otherwise exact fenced answers fail the original format
criterion. In particular, mapped `parent_wide` and one repeated `left_wide`
have exact rows and citations inside the fence but still fail the official
format rule. The other mapped `left_wide` has an incorrect citation, and the
inline control has a null citation instead of `inline-example`; these remain
content/attribution failures even when the rows are present.

This is a diagnostic observation on the same eight outputs per arm, not a new
trial or an unbiased estimate of performance. It motivates a separately
versioned follow-up prompt that explicitly forbids text outside the required
JSON fence. Any such follow-up must retain the strict original scoring rule
and use a new matched preflight before inference. The
[diagnostic artifact](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-output-diagnostic-v1/analysis.json)
stores per-trial official and exploratory scores, output hashes, and the
matched-configuration bindings. No model was loaded or inference run to create
it.

### Strict cited-output protocol v6 preflights

Protocol v6 adds an explicit instruction to return only the required fenced
JSON object. The output constraint is inserted into the final model prompt
after ordinary-chat history retrieval, keeping it out of the retrieval
question. This preserves the original question and selected evidence while
changing only the model-facing response-format instruction. Protocols v1
through v5 retain their original prompt construction and verification rules.
The runner rejects combining v6 with the older single-condition diagnostic,
which has no v6 schema for binding the output contract.

The [control preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-control-strict-preflight-v1/)
and [mapped preflight](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-mapping-strict-preflight-v1/)
each pass the independent v6 preflight verifier. Both use the same pinned
Hermes model identity, Ollama v0.40.16 preflight runtime,
configuration, output contract, user questions, source report, profiles, and
matched prompt bases. Each plans eight trials and exposes five single-record
history conditions. The source evidence is equal across arms; the only planned
intervention is the already-defined explicit history-field-to-row mapping.
The longest planned prompts are 7,828 characters for control and 7,981 for
mapping. The separate [pair verification](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-strict-preflight-pair-v1.json)
checks these bindings and records that inference was not performed and
learning was not established. Checksummed complete captures accompany both
plans.

No model inference was run for v6. The prior Hermes result remains the only
full comparison. A future full pair still needs a matched runtime and strict
full-output scoring; this preflight is not evidence that the new instruction
improves recall or that developmental learning occurred.

### Matched 2×2 response-format and mapping preflight

The previous full Hermes comparison used Ollama v0.12.9, while the v6
preflights bind Ollama v0.40.16. That runtime change would confound any direct
comparison of strict-output scores against the prior full-run scores. To make
the next inference interpretable, a new no-inference matrix now crosses two factors:
strict fenced output on/off and explicit history-to-row mapping on/off. All
four preflights use the same Hermes artifact, Ollama v0.40.16, generation
settings, user questions, source report, scoring profiles, and archived history
evidence. Each arm plans eight trials; all four expose the same five
single-record history conditions.

The [matrix verifier](../../work/verify_sibling_chat_recall_preflight_matrix.py)
checks all four plans together. The refreshed [baseline control](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-control-baseline-preflight-v2/)
and [baseline mapping](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-mapping-baseline-preflight-v2/)
preflights match the refreshed [strict control](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-control-strict-preflight-v2/)
and [strict mapping](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-mapping-strict-preflight-v2/)
preflights on model/runtime/task bindings and source evidence. Mapping is the
only history-treatment difference; the strict instruction is added to the
final model prompt only after retrieval. The [factorial preanalysis record](../../artifacts/experiments/ordinary-chat-retention-20261125/cuda-hermes-recall-factorial-preanalysis-v1.json)
freezes the official full-response exact count as the primary outcome and
fenced-content/format diagnostics as secondary. This is an exploratory,
post-hoc protocol replication, not a confirmatory study; report descriptive
counts only because the eight trials include repeated conditions. The task is
read-only recall, so no learning claim follows from its results. No model
inference was run to prepare this matrix.
All four plans were regenerated after a narrow runner change to permit only
Ollama-generated metadata files named for pinned model layers in the isolated
model store; their source-code hashes therefore bind the same updated runner.

All four plans now bind the intended 24-layer placement for a later full run,
while the preflights themselves load no model. Every new GPU-backed cited run
requires `--expected-plan-sha256`; the runner aborts before generation if its
freshly captured plan differs from the selected preflight. Completed runs
retain a `preflight_binding.json` that the independent full-run or timeout
verifier checks.

The [matrix launcher](../../work/run_sibling_chat_recall_matrix.py) rechecks
all four preflights before it starts, runs them in the frozen order
control → strict+mapping → mapping → strict, verifies each completed arm, and
packages each arm's complete capture and checksum. It stops after the first
failed or unverifiable arm and writes a run manifest after every transition.
After the first verified arm, it permits reuse only when the external Ollama
server has exactly one resident model and its digest matches the pinned
Hermes artifact. That model can remain resident for the configured five-minute
keep-alive after the last arm. The private-store pinning check allows the
runtime metadata generated for pinned layers while rejecting other files.
After all arms pass, it writes a
[matrix verification report](../../work/verify_sibling_chat_recall_factorial_run.py)
that reopens each checksummed capture, regrades every response, and confirms the
four full-run hashes against the frozen preflight matrix. The report is saved
as `matrix-verification.json` beside the run manifest.
The full v2 matrix completed with all four arms independently verified. Its
official exact/valid-format counts were: control 0/8 and 0/8; mapping only
0/8 and 0/8; strict output only 4/8 and 6/8; strict output plus mapping 5/8
and 6/8. Descriptively, strict output coincided with 12/16 valid-format and
9/16 exact responses, compared with 0/16 valid-format and 0/16 exact without
it. The mapping contrast was 5/16 versus 4/16 exact responses. These are
small, repeated-condition counts, not independent-sample estimates. The run
shows a response-format difference under this setup; it does not establish
learning or durable developmental change. The experiment made no history
updates. Its matrix hash is
`b9f710499af1486f75e6399ce9b94ca98e3bb0d3f03b1ab02993cb79e4fc4c90`.

The [factorial output diagnostic](../../work/analyze_sibling_chat_recall_factorial_outputs.py)
regrades the fenced payload separately from the full response, while retaining
official scores unchanged. It operationalizes the preanalysis's secondary
content, citation, and outer-prose diagnostics. The control and mapping-only
arms had correct fenced content plus citation on 2/8 and 7/8 trials despite
0/8 official exact scores in each arm; seven responses in each arm included
outer prose. By comparison, strict control and strict+mapping were 4/8 and
5/8 on both official exact scores and fenced-content-plus-citation scores.
Across the strict-output factor, fenced-content-plus-citation was 9/16 with
strict output and 9/16 without it, while official full-response exactness was
9/16 versus 0/16. This separates wrapper compliance from payload correctness
in this run. Across mapping, the secondary payload score was 12/16 with mapping
and 6/16 without it, a candidate signal that needs fresh, independent task
conditions before it can support a general claim. The report is saved as
`factorial-output-diagnostic.json` beside the verified matrix results.
